Admission/isolation source research · revision 24

Result: read-only inspection of official openai/codex tag rust-v0.167.0 identifies an experimental candidate, not a complete supported or verified topology. R19-F01 remains open. No installation, runtime configuration change, credential access, model call or probe was performed. Source evidence is not runtime behavior or approval to change tool access.

  • Shell registration: features.shell_tool=false prevents shell registration; disabling only unified_exec retains one-shot execution. Native delegation feature keys exist, but disabling native delegation is not by itself admission enforcement. Pinned registration; feature definitions.
  • No public all-tool allowlist established: apply_patch registers independently of shell settings. The internal Rust ToolPolicy.allowed_tools is supplied through extension startup; this research did not establish an equivalent Python/app-server thread-start control. Do not invent one. Patch registration; internal policy.
  • No attached environment candidate: thread/start.environments=[] disables environment access unless a turn overrides it. Tools stay advertised; the environment resolver fails when no usable environment exists, and patch execution passes through that boundary. This is experimental and requires auditing every relevant route rather than relying on tool invisibility. Start contract; retained-tool test; resolver; patch boundary.
  • Callback identity: dynamic calls carry transport-provided thread, turn and call IDs. The public low-level client supports a constructor callback and typed requests over stdio. A controller may compare these IDs with its registered assignment, but argument-supplied roles and raw correlation IDs are not authorization. Transport must stay inaccessible to commands. Wire fields; client.
  • Real child lineage remains unresolved: returned parent, fork and session-tree identities are distinct. Inspected thread-start parameters do not expose an authenticated parent-creation operation. Native spawning checks the real registered parent; starting a fresh SDK thread from a callback does not establish that relationship. Thread identities; start fields; native parent checks.
  • Stop is not release: SDK close terminates the app-server process and escalates to kill, without proving descendant/container cleanup. Turn interruption, terminal termination, command-session termination and unsubscribe have separate meanings. Retain the measured-stop contract. Pinned close; official control APIs.

Candidate for independent review: keep the authenticated runtime and its stdio channel outside all command/test containers; attach no execution environment; explicitly disable built-in shell and native delegation; offer only narrow dynamic operations executed by the controller in credential-free assignment-scoped containers. This is not another framework, a completed design, or permission to weaken Full Access/never defaults. A no-environment restriction is itself a material tool/topology choice requiring explicit review.

Ordered remaining evidence: independently challenge the candidate; statically audit start/turn/resume/fork fallback, hooks/plugins/MCP/extensions, control-channel reachability and credential separation. Then, only with the necessary separate authorization, use an offline pinned fixture with a synthetic sentinel and no model call to inspect effective environment denial, callback identity and cleanup. A newly authorized bounded live experiment is still required for actual model-issued callbacks, allowed/denied creation, owner/reviewer separation, capacity and stop/release. No allowance is requested or consumed by this source report. Direct mode can be considered independently of child lineage but still needs isolation/admission/cleanup. Ordinary and complex modes remain blocked on actual parent creation; complex mode additionally requires genuine Issue Lead-created children.

Independent source challenge A1, 10 November 2026: the pinned synchronous Python client invokes the constructor callback on its sole stdout reader thread. A callback that synchronously invokes request() on that same client can wait for a reply that only the blocked reader can deliver; long callbacks also block response/notification draining. A supported nonblocking callback/control design with independent watchdog/termination handling needs review before this candidate can be accepted. Do not use private client fields as a workaround. Synchronous request wait; reader/callback dispatch. This is source reasoning, not a runtime reproduction. The linked native-parent snippet covers v2 reload/resume validation, not a public authenticated child-creation API. All admission, lifecycle and lineage gates remain open.

Additional independent challenge A2: empty environments remain conditional and experimental. A future design needs an exhaustive tool/extension/hook/MCP and start/turn/resume/fork route matrix, with uncontrolled environment overrides prohibited. The SDK overlays config.env on its inherited process environment, so a small dictionary does not scrub inherited credentials; default command/file approval handling accepts requests. Explicit environment/configuration provenance and rejection of unexpected requests remain necessary. None of these observations establishes a complete safe topology, and no runtime change was made.

Supported control decision before D02 · revision 35, 14 November 2026

Independently reviewed source proposal; recommended source-only topology authorized on 14 November 2026. Continue D00 from the completed local Docker and narrow zero-model SDK observations. Do not implement another disconnected validator or enable execution. A1 is the synchronous SDK reader/callback dependency; A2 is complete tool/route and configuration isolation; A3 is authenticated child-creation lineage. R19-F01 requires those controls together with global admission. None is closed by this proposal.

Missing capability: the inspected pinned Python SDK has no public deferred server-response interface. Its callback receives method/normalized params and must return a result on the sole reader thread; waiting for a same-client request there prevents that reader from delivering the response. Offloading work and then waiting in the callback has the same problem. The async wrapper does not add the missing interface. Separately, the inspected thread-start contract exposes no authenticated parent-creation operation: correlation IDs, client-supplied source labels, forks and resumed native parents do not create authenticated child lineage. A raw protocol transport would not add that capability either.

OptionConsequence and decision
Retain pinned Python SDK for operational controlKeep the maintained refusal helper and completed qualification. Operational dynamic work remains blocked until a public deferred-response/control path and the missing authenticated creation/isolation capability are available and qualified. Do not monkeypatch private fields, issue synchronous reentrant requests, or invent a job-accepted/polling tool protocol to disguise the missing synchronous result.
Proposed direct-mode app-server protocol adapterWithin the existing Python application's SDK-adapter responsibility, use documented private-to-controller stdio JSONL, with an independent reader and serialized writer. Defer dynamic responses while the controller executes admitted work in assignment-scoped containers. No additional framework, daemon, network listener or queue service. This replaces the Python SDK transport for that experimental direct mode; it adds protocol maintenance and failure-handling responsibility. Recommend only a source-first direct-mode evaluation, conditional on explicit authority for this transport/tool topology. It does not qualify ordinary/complex delegation.
Native Rust extension topologyPublic Rust types and local tree shutdown do not establish a Python admission bridge. A Rust host would change the selected architecture and build/dependency scope and still need supported admission coverage and per-owner isolation. Do not adopt it as an incidental repair; no build, install or topology change is authorized here.

Proposed direct-mode request and failure flow

  1. Launch boundary: a protected controller worker owns exactly one app-server process and private stdio pipes. Before launch, validate immutable runtime/configuration provenance and use an explicitly constructed environment, not an overlay on an inherited operator environment. No host project code, hooks or Git configuration executes in that worker. Credential provisioning is a separate prerequisite; no profile copy, sign-in or credential access is authorized by this design.
  2. Tool boundary: request no attached execution environment, disable native shell and delegation, and disable or reject all unneeded extension/plugin/MCP/hook routes through verified supported controls. Advertise only the custom dynamic operation defined below; built-in tools may remain advertised. Inventory every retained built-in separately and require source-supported and measured denial before acceptance. Full Access/never remains the declared permission policy; the proposed tool/environment restriction is an additional material topology choice, not a silent change to the current chat or an already accepted isolation guarantee.
  3. Receive: the wire reader validates the envelope, request ID, method and exact object params before any normalization. Preserve request/response IDs and keep outbound and inbound correlation separate. It never waits for runtime RPC completion, container execution, storage or model work. A bounded in-process handoff sends a valid request to the control loop. Queue saturation, oversized/invalid JSON, duplicate IDs, non-string methods or unknown routes fence admission; protocol-error response is permitted only when the envelope safely supports it, otherwise close the owned transport and reconcile. No default empty-success reply.
  4. Authorize and reserve: the control loop binds the private connection, runtime generation, registered thread/active turn and call ID to its supervisor-issued assignment. Payload roles/IDs alone grant no authority. Recheck owner version, candidate, hold, original deadline and global capacity before atomically persisting intent/reservation and dispatching work. In-memory queues are not durable intent. Existing D03 journals are source primitives, not an integrated transaction or authenticated ledger; the protected transaction/recovery design is still required before dispatch can be implemented.
  5. Execute and respond: only controller-selected operations reach an exact owned container; commands cannot receive controller pipes, Docker sockets, credentials, operator home or state. The independent reader continues draining responses/events while the controller waits for work. The sole writer emits the result for the original inbound request only after the operation's actual outcome is known. Unknown outcomes stay UNKNOWN; no accepted-job response, automatic replay or capacity release. The runtime's request deadlines/cancellation semantics need separate qualification.
  6. Stop and recover: a controller-independent supervisor fences new work and tracks exact runtime/process/container instances. Transport loss, malformed wire input, queue overflow, deadline, worker death or restart preserve spent/reserved allowance and uncertain intent. Reconcile any in-flight create/start before retry, prevent concurrent reload/resume and release capacity only after required process-exit and exact container-deletion observations. The completed local canaries cover only their recorded boundaries; full CP4/5/6 remain open. A new transport's shutdown is not qualified by the old SDK's initialize/close evidence.

Integrated source-first contract for the proposed evaluation

Scope and files: proposed automation/control_transport.py owns raw framing/correlation, automation/control.py owns the state machine, and automation/control_store.py owns its protected SQLite transaction. They remain within the existing adapter/controller responsibilities. Tests connect that actual path to synthetic stdio and supervisor peers; this is not a stand-alone permission validator. No public CLI/startup route constructs a runtime or executor; execution remains disabled. No maintained private-fixture copy, production ledger, SDK replacement elsewhere or migration of existing journals is included.

Exact operation: the only proposed custom dynamic tool is rnr_command, accepting exactly {argv: list[str]}. It has no caller, role, owner, environment, image, mount, cwd, credential, timeout or budget override. The controller supplies those from the protected assignment. Reject empty argv, NUL-containing strings, unknown fields and any request exceeding the configured finite frame/argument limits before handoff; tests use explicit finite limits and no implicit unbounded defaults. An owner can execute project commands only inside its assigned writable source container; a separate check/reviewer assignment receives a frozen candidate with the appropriate restricted mount policy. The command may read/write within that container, so this is no command allowlist or proof of alternate-start prevention. The result is bounded stdout/stderr, exit status and controller-issued evidence reference with explicit success/failure/UNKNOWN. No spawn, network, host-Git, publish, credential, file API or configuration dynamic tool is admitted. Direct mode has no child agent creation.

Controller-only interfaces: register_assignment binds a supervisor-issued assignment and owner epoch to one private connection/generation, registered thread/turn, workspace/candidate, exact resource policy and approved limits. reserve_turn must commit before any outbound turn request; dispatch failure with unknown submission stays charged. receive_request binds the raw request and dynamic call ID to that registration, ignoring claimed identity in arguments. complete_operation records observed result; fence_generation prevents new dispatch and initiates reconciliation; reconcile_allocation records externally observed process/container outcomes. These are controller entry points, not agent-callable tools. Only valid registered direct assignments may reach the synthetic execution seam. Unknown owner numeric versions block operational registration; the historical UNKNOWN ownership record is never coerced into a new numeric grant.

One transaction owner: the store's single serialized worker owns one dedicated SQLite database and transaction boundary covering run deadline/remaining allowances, assignment/epoch/fence state, inbound request identity/hash, intent, reservations and append-only transitions. A logical command is unique by assignment epoch plus thread/turn/call ID; its transport ID is separately scoped to connection/generation. The same identity with different bytes is a conflict; duplicates never execute twice. A transaction checks current fences, holds, known finite allowances and owner/candidate before atomically inserting intent and reservation. Existing operation/hold/usage journal APIs own separate exact schemas/transactions and cannot be nested or presented as this atomic store; retain them unchanged. Any future projection/export is derived after commit, never another admission authority. Source tests cannot establish power-loss durability or authenticated provenance.

Ordering and crash reconciliation: states are reserved → dispatching → result_known, with unknown retained whenever dispatch may have happened. Persist dispatching before handing the operation to the supervisor seam, so a crash in that gap conservatively retains the reservation. Only a durable reserved operation with proof that no dispatch marker/handoff occurred may be cancelled as never submitted. On restart fence the generation first; reconcile every dispatching/unknown operation against the supervisor's exact allocation identity and receipts, with no automatic replay. Results are committed before the response is enqueued. A response lost after result commit is a delivery uncertainty, not permission to rerun. Success/failure does not itself release resources; separate observed cleanup gates release a reservation. A transport reply is not atomic with SQLite commit; preserve both states. No restart may extend the original deadline or reset spent allowances; missing trusted time/usage/fence evidence blocks admission.

Responsive control and containment: container work runs in a separate bounded worker, never on the reader, state-machine loop, store worker or writer. The state machine advances on completion events and permits only one outstanding store mutation; storage waits cannot hold a global lock needed by fencing. The reader uses a bounded nonblocking handoff, and the writer has a bounded output queue plus externally enforced write deadline. A separate supervisor observes worker liveness and the original absolute deadline independently of all these queues and SQLite. Storage stall, full queue, blocked writer or lost heartbeat fences the generation and stops owned allocations through that separate path. The supervisor must serialize fence versus actual launch and reject stale launch tickets immediately before any create/start; a Python boolean, queued cancellation or expired token alone is not a launch fence. If that capability is unavailable, the runtime seam remains disabled. Do not wait for a protocol response to terminate an allocation. Numeric queue/frame/worker bounds and watchdog intervals are explicit test configuration until a separately reviewed runtime contract supplies approved limits.

Schema and compatibility: the saved pinned CLI schemas under the existing private preimplementation evidence root expose experimental environments and dynamicTools; installed generated Python models omit them. Bind synthetic protocol fixtures to those saved raw schemas and hash/provenance records, never regenerate by launching a runtime under exhausted authority. Verify the frozen installed binary accepts the required fields and experimental initialization in a separately authorized zero-model experiment before any operational claim. Current documentation alone cannot establish that match, and a model silently discarding fields is not effective isolation.

Acceptance for this source-only evaluation: trace a valid raw dynamic request through registration, intent/reservation transaction, synthetic supervisor dispatch, committed result and correlated response while another reply/notification is drained. Focused tests must cover malformed/oversized frames, retained built-in refusal, unknown/stale identity, duplicate/conflicting call IDs, exhausted/unknown limits, storage stall, writer backpressure, concurrent fence/launch, crash at each commit/handoff/reply boundary and reconciliation without replay/release. Independent nonauthor source review and QA must examine the integrated path. Synthetic supervisor success proves only the source contract; it cannot close R19-F01, authenticate real callers, qualify admission fences or enable D02.

  3. Fault and recovery acceptance: inject store stalls, writer backpressure, queue saturation, worker death, fence/launch races and interruption at every commit/handoff/reply boundary. A synthetic supervisor must demonstrate the contract's independently responsive fencing seam; it is not evidence of physical enforcement. Restart preserves original deadline, spent/reserved counts and unknown dispatches; no auto replay or release. Include crash-after-result-before-reply and stale epoch completions. All public runner execution/acceptance flags remain false. Tests that only mirror a validator or declare caller-authored authority do not satisfy this step.
  4. Independent gate and publication: freeze the actual changed modules/tests and record hashes; distinct nonauthors review source and independently validate the integrated failure matrix, including omitted-boundary cases. Main manually traces integration, resolves findings through the same source owner and obtains nonauthor recheck. Run relevant existing CLI/journal tests and the full repository suite once for the changed source, then preserve exact results and optional skips. Update only the existing documentation, stage explicit files, validate the commit range, commit/push scoped verified changes and regenerate/check the changelog. A passing synthetic suite never advances D00/TW runtime status.

Proposed fixture: forced deadline with the operator still alive

Question: when a private direct-transport fixture operator stops making progress but remains alive, does the independent watchdog clean up the exact owned allocation at the original deadline, with the known process instances gone and uncertainty preserved? This differs from the already tested dead-parent trigger. It is a lifecycle component probe, not an effectful runner or the outstanding SDK timeout test.

  1. Prepare without execution. Preserve v5 byte-for-byte. A future dedicated implementer owns a new private candidate directory and only its offline tests/receipts; Main owns this plan, assignment and Git. Reuse the pinned canonical harness by hash, not a diverging maintained copy. No changes to automation/control*.py, public CLI, canonical harness or v5 success predicates are proposed. Independently review the new observer, injection and outcome logic before any allowance request.
  2. Proposed finite scope, not a grant. One local WSL2 network-none allocation using the existing immutable image/runtime/harness pins; one app-server process, zero thread/turn submissions, zero dynamic callbacks, zero provider requests/listeners and zero external inference/credentials. Inside it, one fixture root and a synthetic child/grandchild provide a known bounded process tree alongside the idle initialized app-server (four expected in-container process instances; runtime threads are not separately counted processes). Host-side support is one owned fixture operator, one independent watchdog and one bounded observer. No unbounded forks, retries, replacement allocations, image pulls, installation or profile transfer.
  3. Isolation and original time. Retain the reviewed v5 resource policy (UID 65534, read-only root, dropped capabilities/NNP, private namespaces, CPU 1, memory+swap 512 MiB, PID limit 128, scratch 64 MiB) and exact three mount roles: synthetic workspace RW, frozen fixture RO, pinned binary RO. No repository, controller state, credentials or Docker socket inside. Proposed total 240 seconds begins at durable one-shot reservation before operational seams; admission closes at +180, the watchdog cleanup trigger threshold is +210, and all observation/observer teardown ends by +240. The polling loop cannot guarantee action at the exact threshold. Proposed acceptance allows at most two seconds for the independently observed intervention marker after that threshold (by +212), while retaining the original +240 final bound; late detection fails the criterion without cancelling required bounded cleanup. This is a proposed new scope; all old grants remain consumed.
  4. Establish identity before injection. Require watchdog ready PID/session, exact container name/ID/labels/image/policy, successful direct initialization and running synthetic tree. Bind each expected container/host process to PID plus start-time/parent evidence and the private run marker through the supported same-host observation route. Missing, extra or ambiguous process instances mean failure/UNKNOWN, not a guessed match. Retain bounded, nonsecret ready receipts. Source/offline preparation must define exact observation queries and limits; no arbitrary process/environment dump.
  5. Stall only the owned operator. By proposed +30 seconds, the observer may SIGSTOP its own exact fixture-operator child only after a durable quiescent checkpoint confirms all create/start acknowledgements, no pending Docker mutation, no held control lock and no future create/start work. Record the exact stopped instance and leave it alive; do not kill the watchdog, container runtime, main chat or unrelated processes. If readiness is absent, abort injection and perform bounded ordinary cleanup; that is NOT RUN for the forced-deadline criterion. No queued launch or resumption is permitted during the stall.
  6. Observe independent cleanup. The fixture workloads deliberately remain alive beyond the inner admission deadline; no more work may be admitted. The watchdog's original +210 trigger threshold must lead to an independently observed intervention marker by +212, without operator progress or an early parent-death trigger. Record the original monotonic threshold, last marker-absent and first marker-present observations, and cleanup completion; observation brackets are not exact trigger timestamps. Verify the same stopped operator instance is still alive at intervention and no normal-finish receipt exists, excluding parent loss/normal completion as the trigger. Reject observed early or late intervention; the full cleanup and observer teardown must finish by +240. Require a deadline-intervention receipt, exact-ID kill/terminal reinspection/removal, empty exact-ID and run-label inventories, and absence of every recorded process instance. Observe watchdog exit independently; this intervention path has no normal watchdog-finished.json acknowledgement. The observer kills/reaps only its still-stopped child after the intervention result, bounded by +240, and records that ordering. If the watchdog dies or cleanup is uncertain, preserve UNKNOWN and forbid automatic retry/extension.
  7. Separate fault outcome from qualification. Keep the underlying watchdog/interrupted workload status UNKNOWN and capacity unreleased by the runner. A separate immutable observation report may say forced_deadline_observed=true only when all exact timing/identity/cleanup assertions pass. Never make v5 check_inner accept an incomplete run or remove its intervention failure rule. Store all original times, grant reservation, injection/ready/command receipts, process identities/exits and reviewer evidence; record monotonic completion directly rather than relying only on file modification time.

Required offline preparation and abort coverage: inspect the complete ready→quiesce→stop→deadline→cleanup→reap flow before tests. Independently challenge PID reuse, wrong run labels, incomplete/extra tree inventory, early parent death, missing readiness, pending create/start, held lock, watchdog death, missing intervention result, failed removal/nonempty inventory, observer death, oversized/malformed receipts and original-deadline exhaustion. Use inert boundaries, not a live process/socket/container merely to prepare. Stop/reap operations must be identity-bound and idempotent without replaying work. An independent outer recovery path for observer failure and bounded observer exit must be settled in source review; an unproved teardown path blocks launch. Freeze exact new source hashes, limits, receipt schemas and launcher before presenting the actual allowance request. This document deliberately issues no grant and is not launch-ready.

Acceptance limit: even a passing observation closes only this direct-fixture forced-deadline containment subcase. It cannot establish a real serialized launch fence: current harness cleanup can bypass a held lock after a bounded wait, and a previously in-flight Docker create/start could still finish late. The proposed injection excludes that race rather than claiming to solve it. Lost-acknowledgement/controller-restart qualification must follow a separately reviewed supervisor authority contract that prevents or conservatively reconciles late effects under the original deadline. No host service, token broker, bespoke sandbox, API or native lineage capability is invented here.

Main owns contracts, canonical documents and Git; no branches/worktrees, installations, credentials, globals or unrelated operations.

Recovery contract FD-1a (supersedes FD-1 count): four persistent host processes form bounded caller → guardian/watchdog → observer → operator. Caller reserves the original allowance before fork; guardian stays mutation-free until caller binds its identity/pidfd and explicitly releases it. All scope/templates/receipts must declare four, including the caller. Guardian sets/readbacks Linux child-subreaper before spawning; operator sets/readbacks parent-death SIGKILL between expected-parent checks before any mutation, with no later credential transitions or exec. Use pidfds plus PID/start-time/parent/run receipts for signals. Guardian binds both descendants before entering the unchanged canonical watchdog; observer releases the parked operator only after verifying guardian watchdog readiness. No Docker operation precedes this gate. The operator quiesces irrevocably before injection, with no pending mutation/held lock/future launch. The observer never resumes the stopped operator.

The durable allowance keeps +180 admission, +210 cleanup threshold, +212 intervention-observation limit and +240 overall end. The wrapper explicitly supplies a separate +235 command subdeadline to canonical watchdog state, leaving nominal teardown headroom; this is not an extension or a claim of exact scheduling. Canonical watchdog return is recorded separately from guardian process exit, avoiding a circular wait: observer checks cleanup, kills/reaps its owned operator, writes its observation and exits; guardian reaps observer, adopts/reaps orphaned operator if necessary, and emits final UNKNOWN workload status plus separate qualified observation. Guardian recovery kills only pidfd-bound descendants, never resumes operator. Observer death triggers operator parent-death kill and guardian adoption; observer hang is recovered by guardian after canonical cleanup. Either invalidates forced-deadline qualification. Failure paths remain UNKNOWN/no replay; measured completion after +240 fails acceptance.

Caller completion: caller independently records guardian exit but cannot prove its own subsequent exit. Its report is provisional; final forced_deadline_observed=true requires the invoking tool to observe caller exit within the original +240 bound and a separate immutable reconciliation record. No fifth fixture supervisor is added. Missing/late invoking-tool evidence leaves qualification UNKNOWN. Caller death permits already reserved guardian cleanup under original times, never retry; orphan reaping then belongs to external ancestry and is unproved. Caller cutoff must record bounded signal/reap outcomes and unresolved descendants/commands, never claim successful recovery. Source review identified startup bind failure, pre-output completion timestamps and swallowed cutoff outcomes; these must close before fixture acceptance.

Explicit boundary: guardian death during an unchanged harness Docker command can leave a command/mutation uncertain. Observer may stop admission, kill/reap its operator and attempt bounded identity-bound cleanup, but cannot claim complete automatic command recovery. Simultaneous supervisor failure, kernel stalls and scheduling bounds are not guaranteed. The fixture must record these limits, fail closed on watchdog loss, and never credit the intended subcase on recovery. This does not implement the general serialized launch fence. Linux source references: parent-death signal, child subreaper and pidfd signaling. Independent source consultation accepted this direction with the stated limitations; code remains to be reviewed.

Exact source contract, wire subset, state transitions and acceptance cases
Decision and exact property

Allow arbitrary ordinary project code and its local subprocesses within a bounded untrusted workload allocation. Do not count every interpreter or offline agent-shaped program as a managed agent. Instead require that project code cannot obtain or exercise protected runner capabilities except through the assigned admitted operation. Managed agents are supervisor/controller-registered AI sessions whose model work, identity, budget, creation, wake and termination are charged and governed by the runner. A process calling itself an agent has no such authority.

Protected capabilities are:

  1. Model/provider authority: credentials, account/session tokens, local credential helpers, authenticated proxies, provider endpoint access and routes to internal inference services. Any model invocation authorized by the runner belongs to a reserved managed turn. Workload-originated network inference is denied, including public unauthenticated services; controlling only credential files is insufficient.
  2. Controller authority: private app-server protocol input/output, registration and turn APIs, durable SQLite/journals/leases, candidate identity, assignment epochs, policy/configuration, grant/allowance state, controller memory and process handles. Workload output is data, never a serialized administrative request.
  3. Supervisor authority: container/process creation outside the assigned workload, stop/fence/reconcile controls, exact allocation identity and host management sockets, host namespace/cgroup mutation and release observations. Caller-authored process-exited/deleted flags are never physical evidence.
  4. Managed creation and wake: thread start/resume/fork, turn start/steer/continuation, queue/goal/realtime/review/compaction routes that can cause model work, native agent spawn/followup/resume/mailbox wake, and equivalent extensions. Direct mode denies managed child creation. Ordinary/complex mode remains blocked on authenticated assigning-session lineage (A3), independent of this change.

Bounded subprocesses may start offline runtimes inside the allocation. This is an explicit change in intended property, not a claim historical alternate-start denial passed. Existing resource, deadline, role separation, host-Git and cleanup requirements remain. Reject a command allowlist as the primary isolation measure: a permitted project build tool can execute project scripts. Reject adding another standalone validator: admission must connect to actual controller state and transport.

Allocation and capability separation

Retain existing Controller/Store/Transport/Supervisor responsibilities. The proposed trusted control allocation contains the controller, protected store and private protocol endpoint; its app-server/model component is separately isolated from project execution and receives only the narrowly needed provider route. The untrusted workload allocation contains the source/candidate, bounded scratch and project processes. An owner gets its authorized writable source mount; an independent reviewer gets the frozen candidate under its distinct policy. Neither shares the control process namespace or writable protected state.

Supervisor construction must use a fresh explicit environment, closed inherited descriptors and explicit stdio pipes; no SDK environment overlay assumption. Exclude provider keys/tokens, HOME credential/config trees, SSH/Git helpers, cloud metadata access, host /proc, controller database/lockfiles, app-server/agent/control pipes, Docker/containerd/Kubernetes/SSH-agent sockets, host devices and privileged namespace/cgroup access. No host network or privileged mounts. Use immutable runtime/config pins, read-only base filesystem where supported, bounded PID/memory/CPU/storage/output and a deadline enforced outside the workload. Workload descendants remain in the same independently killable allocation.

Workload egress defaults to none, including loopback routes to privileged services, DNS/proxy escape and reachable local inference. If future builds need dependency downloads, prefer pre-staged immutable dependencies; adding a narrowly restricted artifact route is a separately designed boundary and must not become a general HTTP proxy. Credentials must not enter source mounts or command output. The trusted app-server also needs complete native/extension/tool/config closure: its provider access would otherwise be available to a bypass route even when the workload is isolated.

These are required runtime controls, not current Supervisor behavior: tests/test_automation_control.py::Supervisor is explicitly synthetic. Current Assignment.allocation is a workload identity; do not silently reuse it as both workload and control identity. A future runtime registration must bind distinct identities and their provenance. The next source slice below does not add a false second allocation claim.

Configuration and protocol policy

Construct immutable effective policy from protected inputs. No project/user/agent overrides may select model/provider, environment, roots, MCP servers, plugins, hooks/notify, tools, network, approval/permissions, sandbox, budgets or native delegation. Exact allowed effective configuration must be checked after all upstream layers and overrides, not merely hashed from submitted TOML. Unknown layers, fields, routes or registration sources block the mode.

For the next slice, outbound effectful allowlist is exactly turn/start for an already protected, registered thread; initialization/thread registration remains a test prerequisite and no thread/start, resume, fork or creation implementation is added. Controller constructs all params from protected assignment/policy plus bounded plain-text task input. Reject a generic method/params passthrough and reject user-provided environment, thread, model, permission, configuration or budget overrides. Before implementation, bind the exact request/response subset to the saved pinned schemas, including the server-issued turn ID; do not guess that a client-selected reservation ID is a server turn ID.

Inbound allowlist remains correlated responses, item/tool/call with exact rnr_command {argv} and the already-supported turn/completed observation. Unknown notifications/requests fence by default. A completion notification is not a release receipt. Other protocol route families identified in the A2 inventory are denied/unreachable until individually classified. Controller-level allowlisting does not prove app-server internal tools/wake routes cannot bypass it.

One next vertical outcome: durable outbound turn admission through the existing controller

Outcome: a trusted synthetic caller requests one model turn, the same Store atomically charges it before any bytes can be emitted, Transport sends one exact allowed frame, Controller correlates its reply and binds the returned server turn ID before admitting rnr_command; any uncertainty stays charged and cannot replay. This closes a concrete source gap: current reserve_turn() only documents that callers must wait before sending, while Transport.send() accepts arbitrary messages and outbound responses are only placed in observed.

Keep Controller.__init__ raising and execution_enabled=False. Retain for_synthetic and synthetic supervisor checks. No operational launcher, provider call, new runtime process, SDK usage, CLI flag or public activation. In v2, both Controller.reserve_turn and the Store reserve_turn mutation route must reject legacy calls. Migrate existing synthetic helpers to start_turn followed by a correlated server reply and committed binding; add explicit legacy-API refusal regressions. No mixed legacy active-turn mode or client-chosen server turn ID is permitted.

Integrated interfaces and state
  • Add controller-only start_turn(assignment, intent_id, text) (names may follow existing conventions). IDs and input are explicitly bounded; no arbitrary dict options. The controller retrieves trusted registered thread and connection/generation; it constructs the fixed request. A duplicate intent with identical input returns its durable status without sending again; conflicting bytes reject/fence. Assignment epoch is part of identity.
  • Extend the existing Store, not another journal, with turn intents and append-only transitions. Unique logical identity is assignment + epoch + intent ID; record request fingerprint, connection/generation, controller-issued wire ID, submission state and nullable server turn ID. Persist only bounded task data needed for the contract; prefer a digest and protected in-memory text since replay is prohibited. The charge and reserved intent commit atomically against original deadline, hold/fence state, global/per-assignment turn limits and at most one unresolved/active turn per assignment. Do not reuse command run.active as a model-turn limit: its current meaning is unreconciled command allocations; pending turns need their own explicitly bounded accounting if globally limited by concurrency.
  • Commit dispatching before enqueue; the controller then registers expected response and enqueues the exact frame in a controlled Transport method. Type-distinguished wire IDs remain generation-scoped. Queue acceptance is enqueued, never provider acceptance. Persist dispatch marker before any write. A store/queue/write failure after the marker is UNKNOWN and fences; no refund or resend. Registration/enqueue failure with zero emitted bytes still consumes the turn conservatively.
  • A validated turn/start reply binds its server turn ID transactionally, only to that outstanding protected intent and registered thread. A malformed/error response resolves to an explicit failed/UNKNOWN state as appropriate but retains the charge. The next command must match this committed binding. A callback arriving before the binding is committed must not execute; the minimal slice may fence it as unsupported ordering, documenting that this conservative behavior needs real protocol-order qualification. Do not silently accept a caller-supplied ID to avoid this race.
  • Successful reply means turn accepted, not turn finished or allocation released. A correctly matched completion observation may end the logical active-turn state only after its bounded schema is validated; it cannot release command capacity. Unrelated notifications cannot permit another turn. An uncertain turn requires fencing/reconciliation before further activity; terminal model result does not refund cumulative usage.
  • Use the current single pending-store mutation loop and independent reader/writer. Add response handling to _run without blocking the reader on Store or command completion. Correlation state must be bounded; duplicate/stale/unregistered responses cannot bind another turn. Preserve observed response delivery where existing tests need it, without treating the observation queue as admission authority.
  • The writer must refuse newly dequeued effectful frames once the generation is fenced. Real serialization of fence versus physical transmission requires the eventual trusted transport/supervisor boundary; a pre-send Python flag alone is not proof. An already in-flight/partial write is uncertain, never replayed, and independent fencing must close its owned channel. Synthetic tests must expose the race rather than claim physical prevention from booleans.
  • Reload fences recovered registrations before starting threads, preserves spent counts, marks dispatching/enqueued/unresolved accepted turns uncertain as appropriate, and never emits a recovered request. A new generation cannot inherit dispatch authority from an old row. Existing command recovery/reconcile behavior remains.
Exact implementation files and compatibility

automation/control.py: controller-only start-turn entry, serialized reservation/dispatch/reply/binding state machine, bounded in-flight bookkeeping and fence integration. automation/control_store.py: turn-intent schema, admission/dispatch/bind/terminal/recovery transactions and snapshot evidence. automation/control_transport.py: fixed outbound request builder/controlled enqueue + generation-aware queued-send refusal and existing correlation integration. Use helpers here rather than a new disconnected policy module unless code size justifies a tightly integrated helper.

tests/test_automation_control.py: real controller/store/transport integrated tests using disposable SQLite and synthetic stdio/supervisor peers. tests/test_automation_control_transport.py: focused wire builder, exact allowlist and queue/correlation tests. No real process, network, Docker or model calls. Keep database schema checks exact. Increment source-evaluation database version; reject old private v1 databases without modification, with an explicit unsupported-version diagnostic. No production migration or rewrite of unrelated journals. Existing v1 evidence remains historical; fresh v2 disposable stores are appropriate for this source-only slice.

Accepted shared-interface corrections

TA-API-2 refines the same source contract. Reservation returns an explicit fresh/existing/conflict disposition; only the transaction that inserts and charges a new intent can return fresh dispatch authority. A bounded identical retry returns durable status in every state, including fenced/recovered state, without another charge, candidate wire ID, correlation or send. Logical equality excludes a new candidate wire ID. Conflicting input commits a registration fence before returning a conflict disposition; the controller then rejects the caller without rolling back that fence.

Conflicting terminal observations commit bounded prior/received kind and fingerprint evidence while preserving original terminal history and its one-time logical decrement. Keep one first-contradiction row per intent and a saturating observation count; no raw payloads. A negative-only ingress check against committed terminal fingerprints fences immediately even while another Store mutation stalls. Deferred evidence uses the same serialized mutation slot; pending/failed persistence stays visible rather than being reported as reconciled. A later active intent becomes UNKNOWN with its slot retained. The released logical count never proves physical capacity release. Exact API shapes and task ownership remain in the reviewed private contract, with the original proposal preserved as history.

Independent acceptance contract

Trace the implemented flow manually before testing. Independently inspect and test: (1) charge visible in Store before sink sees bytes; (2) one allowed request → correlated server reply → committed ID → admitted command → committed result/reply; (3) another reply/notification drains during blocked store/command work; (4) all unsupported methods and config/identity overrides fail before emission; (5) duplicate/conflicting intent and response IDs cannot resend or rebind; (6) exhausted/unknown limits, held/fenced/stale assignments and changed deadline cannot send; (7) crash/reload at reservation, marker, enqueue, partial-write, reply and bind boundaries retains charges and emits nothing; (8) storage stall, saturated queues, writer failure and fence/send race become fenced/UNKNOWN without refund/release; (9) early callback never launches; (10) command capacity is not released by turn replies/completion; (11) source constructor remains disabled and v1 database is preserved on refusal.

Fresh nonauthor review and QA must assess the integrated changed snapshot and negative tests; an independent reviewer must explicitly challenge protected versus untrusted input boundaries and UNKNOWN/replay ordering. Passing source tests advances only this source contract. It does not close A2, R19-F01, A3, physical admission/lifecycle, any full TW scenario or D02 enablement.

Exact saved-schema wire and interface contract

Same assignment/ownership v1, at supervisor request. This appendix resolves the earlier “bind before implementation” prerequisite and narrows the source slice deliberately. It is an allowlisted subset of saved schemas, not a complete app-server client or a claim that live output conforms to the subset. Read-only JSON inspection and SHA256 calculation performed; no imports of candidate code, tests, regeneration, network or runtime. Protected policy and exact request

Add an immutable source-only TurnPolicy supplied by the trusted constructor, with explicit model and effort strings (each 1–256 UTF-8 bytes, no control characters) plus explicit finite input byte and item/text bounds. No default model, no caller-selected effort, and no hardcoded claim about actual model capabilities. The saved ReasoningEffort definition is a nonempty string, not a fixed enum; the protected policy must own the already-reviewed exact advertised choice and parent ceilings. Synthetic values are declared test values. No model is contacted to validate them.

Persist the canonical policy fingerprint as part of the v2 Store's protected run configuration and compare it on reload. The controller constructor requires exactly that policy. This avoids switching model/effort while retaining an old reservation. The mutable caller cannot replace a dict after validation: dataclass scalars/frozen values, defensive construction and Store revalidation own the contract. Policy is shared for this bounded source evaluation; per-assignment heterogeneous model policies are out of scope. Add the policy to the v2 Store/Controller factory contract, preserving the operational constructor prohibition.

start_turn(assignment_id, intent_id, text) accepts only bounded identifiers and one Python string. Require nonempty valid UTF-8 text within input_bytes; allow ordinary newline/tab, reject NUL and invalid surrogates. The controller builds exactly:

{"id":"<controller-issued-generation-scoped-wire-id>","method":"turn/start","params":{"threadId":"<registered-thread>","input":[{"type":"text","text":"<bounded-task-text>","text_elements":[]}],"environments":[],"approvalPolicy":"never","sandboxPolicy":{"type":"dangerFullAccess"},"model":"<protected-policy-model>","effort":"<protected-policy-effort>","runtimeWorkspaceRoots":[]}}

The JSON placeholders above are explanatory, not literal fixtures. The exact params-key set is the eight keys shown: threadId, input, environments, approvalPolicy, sandboxPolicy, model, effort, runtimeWorkspaceRoots. input has exactly one object with exactly type, text, text_elements; text_elements must be the empty array. No images, URLs as typed attachments, skills, mentions, custom elements, toolOutput, additionalContext, permissions profile, cwd, collaborationMode, parent/root attribution, disabledPluginIds, service-tier mutation or arbitrary extra fields. A URL written in plain text remains text; no controller fetch follows it. approvalPolicy=never means no escalation approval path, not a claim of blanket tool disablement. Explicit sandboxPolicy:{"type":"dangerFullAccess"} preserves the declared Full Access policy for this source wire contract. The saved schema's DangerFullAccessSandboxPolicy variant defines only type, required and fixed to dangerFullAccess; the builder emits exactly that object, with no networkAccess field. This is a submitted policy, not measured isolation. Explicit empty environments, native-route denial, protected-channel isolation and physical workload network denial remain separate required controls; Full Access does not establish or replace them. permissions is omitted because the schema forbids combining it with sandboxPolicy. environments:[] explicitly disables environment access for the turn; omitted/null would inherit sticky environments. runtimeWorkspaceRoots:[] explicitly replaces those roots. multiAgentMode is omitted because the schema marks it ignored/deprecated; sending it cannot enforce native-agent denial.

Existing registered-thread/bootstrap policy must independently exclude collaboration overrides, hooks/plugins/MCP/native creation and other sticky fields. Omitting their turn fields is not evidence of safe inherited state. This slice uses a trusted synthetic registration precondition and keeps that runtime gap visible.

No external method/params sender is exposed through Controller. Transport's generic internals are trusted in-process machinery, not a security boundary against Python code running in the controller process. The workload must never import/call them or hold their descriptors. The controlled outbound builder accepts a Store-authorized ticket and bounded task text whose hash matches the committed fingerprint, not a caller dict; the ticket includes assignment/epoch, connection/generation, thread, intent, wire ID, policy fingerprint and deadline. The exact encoded envelope must fit Bounds.frame_bytes before reservation; encoded frame failure after dispatch marker is UNKNOWN.

Closed response and completion subset

Use existing raw JSON decoder protections (duplicate keys, nonfinite numbers, byte limit) and exact key checks. All protocol string IDs here are strings, 1–256 UTF-8 bytes, without C0/C1 controls or NUL; bool/int IDs are forbidden for server thread/turn IDs. The schema describes Codex turn IDs as UUIDv7 but only constrains them as strings; do not invent UUID enforcement for synthetic compatibility. Wire correlation IDs keep existing type-distinguished rules and must match the controller-issued expected ID.

Success envelope: exactly {id,result}, where result is exactly {turn}. Notification envelope: exactly {method,params}, method exactly turn/completed, params exactly {threadId,turn}. Notification threadId must equal protected registration. Successful response has no threadId in this saved schema; infer its assignment/thread only from outstanding private correlation, never from a fabricated field.

For both objects, turn requires exactly the required keys id, items, status plus any subset of these saved optional keys: error, itemsView, rootTurnId, startedAt, completedAt, durationMs. Reject other keys. Validate:

  • status is exactly inProgress, completed, failed or interrupted; turn/completed excludes inProgress.
  • items is an array bounded by an explicit positive turn_items limit. Narrow source subset: empty or plain agent-message items only. Each item requires exactly id, type, text, optionally delivery, memoryCitation, phase, questions; type is agentMessage, ID uses the bounds above, text is valid UTF-8 within explicit item_text_bytes (empty allowed). Optional fields in this narrow subset must be null. Reject every other ThreadItem variant or non-null optional extension. This admits a minimal textual result without implementing all tool/history payloads. Such rejection fences/UNKNOWN; it is an explicit live-compatibility limitation.
  • itemsView, if present, is exactly notLoaded, summary or full; notLoaded requires empty items. Absence follows the saved default full for interpretation only; do not rewrite raw payload fingerprints.
  • rootTurnId, if present, is null or a bounded ID; it is observation only and grants no lineage.
  • Time/duration fields, if present, are null or exact Python integers within signed 64-bit range; booleans and floats reject. Negative duration rejects. They are untrusted observations and never replace the controller's monotonic deadline or charge clock.
  • error is absent/null or a bounded closed object requiring message and optionally additionalDetails, codexErrorInfo, misalignment. Message is valid UTF-8 within item_text_bytes; additionalDetails is null or equally bounded text. In this narrow subset codexErrorInfo and misalignment must be null. Unknown variants reject rather than being interpreted. inProgress and completed require absent/null error; failed/interrupted may carry the described error. Never log raw error text as execution authority or expose credentials without the established output-redaction policy.

A JSON-RPC {id,error} response is a submission uncertainty: do not interpret an unpinned generic error body as proof no work started. Bound it by the existing frame decoder, correlate ID, fence and retain UNKNOWN/charge; do not persist its arbitrary body. The normal success/notification subset is closed as specified above.

The first valid start response must return a server turn ID never previously bound to another intent on that assignment/epoch. Reject reuse even if the prior turn is terminal: turn/start can steer an existing active turn, and accepting a reused ID as a new independent turn would hide that mismatch. Correlation prevents two different assignments from sharing authority; global uniqueness of arbitrary server IDs is not asserted.

Active-turn and terminal semantics

Use states reserved -> dispatching -> accepted -> terminal, with unknown reachable from every uncertain post-marker boundary. Keep submission observation enqueued as a separate delivery field, not evidence of acceptance. Persist terminal kind completed|failed|interrupted separately. A terminal result arriving in the start response may atomically bind ID and record terminal directly. Before accepting either terminal source, validate the full bounded subset and correlation. A transport error or malformed/ambiguous reply is UNKNOWN, not failed.

At most one nonterminal intent exists per assignment. Reserved/dispatching/accepted/unknown all block another turn. start_turn admission additionally retains the current Store rule that unreleased command allocations block the next turn. Terminal permits a later new intent only when there are no unreleased command allocations and the assignment remains otherwise admissible; current reconciliation may fence the assignment, so terminal does not promise reuse. No cumulative turn charge is refunded. UNKNOWN holds the assignment until containment/reconciliation under a later explicitly designed recovery path; this slice cannot clear UNKNOWN into new dispatch authority.

A completion must match the committed active server turn ID, assignment thread and generation. A completion before start-response binding is unsupported and fences to UNKNOWN. Identical repeated terminal observations may be ignored idempotently using a retained bounded fingerprint; conflicting terminal kind/payload or mismatched turn ID fences. A late response/notification never revives a recovered/fenced assignment. Commands arriving while reserved/dispatching (before binding), after terminal, or for a different turn reject/fence. Admitted command completion may still be recorded after turn terminal; it never retroactively authorizes another command or releases capacity without existing external reconciliation.

For a concrete global admission contract, extend v2 Run with explicit positive turn_capacity and a separate transactional active_turns counter. Reservation increments it atomically and rejects exhaustion; a validated terminal observation decrements it exactly once, whereas UNKNOWN retains the slot. The counter bounds outstanding logical model turns, not physical allocations; terminal still releases no command/container capacity. Require explicit values in synthetic fixtures, no operational default and no inference from command worker count or run.active. This is source bookkeeping, not global managed-agent physical-capacity qualification. The existing Run.turns remains the nonrefundable cumulative allowance. The durable admission owner owns the frozen TurnPolicy type in control_store.py and its canonical fingerprint validation; transport/control consume it without maintaining a second policy definition.

Serialization with the existing loop

Reserve and dispatch marker are separate serialized Store actions with the current _pending slot. Only the marker-commit callback can register correlation/enqueue the prebuilt frame. In-memory intent tracking is populated before enqueue so an immediate peer reply has an owner. A send failure fences and schedules a serialized UNKNOWN mutation; it cannot overwrite an existing _pending. On each loop iteration, finish the pending mutation first; queue wire observations in the existing bounded transport queue meanwhile. A response initiates bind/terminal mutation and the loop does not consume a following command until this commit returns. This permits a reply followed immediately by callback to work without a new blocking reader or second DB owner. Callback preceding response is deliberately rejected, not buffered into authority. Fence has precedence over later binds/admissions, and durable Store methods recheck it. Unknown-state persistence may wait for a stalled DB, but independent supervisor fencing must not wait for it.

Assignment sizing and ownership

This is complex integrated work across three modules, transaction recovery and asynchronous transport, so recommend a fresh Issue Lead under the existing RNR-D00 implementation task, with at least two substantive child owners created by that Lead. Do not split it into unrelated issues or have the main Lead absorb child implementation.

  1. Durable admission owner: exclusive automation/control_store.py and a new focused tests/test_automation_control_store_turns.py; implements immutable policy pinning, v2 schema, bounded turn intents, transactional state/limits/binding/recovery and snapshots against the agreed Store API.
  2. Control/wire owner: exclusive automation/control.py, automation/control_transport.py, tests/test_automation_control.py, tests/test_automation_control_transport.py; implements exact wire subset and integrated state machine against that API, including end-to-end failure/race tests. Those two source modules belong to one owner to avoid cross-module interleaving of lifecycle changes.

Issue Lead first freezes Store method names/return records and TurnPolicy ownership, then schedules these owners with clear API dependencies; transport/parser implementation may proceed independently while store work is underway. Lead owns coordination and integrated acceptance, main owns canonical docs/Git, and a fresh independent reviewer/QA examines the combined snapshot. With four available slots, run parent + Issue Lead + two children for implementation, then stop children before reviewer/QA waves; do not exceed capacity or skip independence. This report does not spawn or assign those agents. All follow-up writes are stopped.

Supervisor checkpoint clarification received before handoff: independent design challenge is mandatory before source writes. The historical Issue Lead counter is UNKNOWN and must not be fabricated/reset; if this hierarchy is adopted, use an explicitly UNKNOWN successor label plus a unique supported task identity and record the counter limitation. This does not grant an operational owner version. The bounded implementation remains one turn-start path for a pre-registered synthetic thread, one plain text input, a deliberately small response subset and durable no-replay accounting. It excludes bootstrap, runtime policy discovery, real supervisor allocation, full ThreadItem/error decoding, protected-capability runtime enforcement and recovery that resumes execution. Early callback/completion mismatch is conservative fencing with retained charge; it is explicitly not runtime acceptance. The design/implementation review should reject scope expansion into those excluded capabilities.

Next real-runtime integration · design in progress

Retain Docker and the accepted Store/client/receiver authority. Killing a timed-out Docker client does not establish daemon no-effect. The proposed additional backstop is one immutable, non-dumpable guardian that remains namespace PID1 and owns a bounded app-stdio relay. It refuses late launch against an original Store-owned absolute CLOCK_BOOTTIME deadline and exits on deadline, app exit, input loss or fatal relay failure. Namespace init exit causes descendant termination; it is not a hard-real-time guarantee under host/kernel/scheduling failure. Already committed start may materialize after fence, within the original allowance; late inert container resources remain UNKNOWN/held until exact reconciliation. No stronger cancellation claim or automatic release is added.

Pre-code requirements from independent architecture assessment: immutable original boot/time-namespace/clock binding established before effects, never recomputed from restart/start time; genuine private PID1 with no init shim or daemon restart policy; read-back non-dumpable state and no project-controlled imports; child FD allowlist excluding timer/management descriptors; absolute timer armed before child release; bounded nonblocking queues, per-event work and deadline priority; trusted lifecycle framing separate from arbitrary app bytes; explicit versioned guardian image/entrypoint/build provenance while preserving fixed child app argv and existing caps-none/NNP/resource policy; original Store dispatch+claim before daemon calls and authoritative late-effect/cleanup observations afterward. A concrete typed clock/image/entrypoint and I/O contract remains required before code.

Read-only host exploration found existing bwrap and delegated cpu/memory/pids controllers, but no alternative-runtime or daemon migration was selected or executed. Prefer the existing topology unless its concrete requirements cannot be met. Source assessment: private reviewer/real-runtime-supervision-design-assessment.md. Primary kernel references: PID namespace init, non-dumpable state, absolute timerfd, clock namespace offsets. No implementation or operational acceptance of this proposal; full D00/A2/A3 gates remain open.
