Outcome and topology

Bind one fresh durable Store handoff to one bounded daemon submission, then preserve uncertainty and held capacity until independent physical reconciliation. Retain the current Docker recipe, guardian and parsers. Recommend one trusted management process containing the sole Store worker and bounded dispatch/receiver/executor threads. Store owns its SQLite connection on its existing worker; executor threads use submit/Future, never SQLite directly. RuntimeDispatchClient and RuntimeDispatchReceiver currently require that exact Store object, so their endpoints may be colocated in this process. Channel credentials/pidfd then authenticate the management process, not isolation between trusted threads. No project code, plugins, app output or provider content executes inside that process.

A separate host containment process is started by trusted bootstrap with explicit descriptor allowlists and its own reviewed finite negative authority. It has no Store object, database lock/connection, positive launch API or general Docker proxy. Establish it before constructing Store, or use an exec/spawn route that proves no Store/management descriptors survive; never fork a live Store into another process. Its observations return to the sole Store for reconciliation. Its finite local inventory is a negative safety backstop, not a second admission, receipt or release authority.

Alternatives: a separate positive executor process requires a new authenticated Store transport because the existing receiver accepts only the exact Store; defer that additional protocol until an established isolation need requires it. A second Store/SQLite opener or inherited Store across fork violates sole ownership and is rejected. Colocation is the proposed smallest compatible topology, not proof that host credentials, management process protection or hostile same-UID isolation already work. If the selected host cannot protect this process, its socket/files and containment from the app, this mode stays disabled rather than weakening the recipe.

Authority at the final send boundary

Existing runtime_action atomically retains commitment/envelope; client sends only its fresh Future result. Existing runtime_claim consumes the exact retained envelope once; receiver abandons late completions. These accepted semantics remain. A serialized RuntimeDispatch, equal object, disk checkpoint or parser result never authorizes an effect. Executor is private to the owning receiver thread and accepts only that thread's current fresh pending claim; no public execute(record), injectable real-effect callback, automatic retry or restart-resume entry point is added.

Proposed additional Store transition, subject to independent design review: a runtime_effect_begin(request, stage) transaction in the existing transitions journal before each permission-bearing substep. Closed stages are create for a create submission, and attach then start for a start submission; no arbitrary action or stage is accepted. It requires the exact already-claimed envelope/epoch, unreleased allocation, unchanged policy/configuration, current positive authority, no fence/cleanup/closure/intervening contradictory receipt, no result/resolution for the submission and no prior effect-begin transition for that exact stage. The start stage additionally requires the same submission's earlier attach-stage transition; a create submission has no attach stage. It checks the retained original clock through control_runtime_clock.check(positive=True), as well as the existing wall-clock checks, then commits runtime_effect_begun keyed by the original submission and closed stage, with Store-derived correlation/sequence. These are finite substeps of the existing DispatchCommit, not extra DispatchCommit actions or a second release inventory. Only its fresh successful Future acknowledgement permits the receiver's still-pending in-memory submission to proceed. No new ticket, reservation, duration, SQLite authority or reusable token is created. A lost/late acknowledgement consumes this permission without sending from a callback; capacity remains held.

This extra phase closes the preparation interval between claim and mutation: image/source checks, exact-ID inspect and attach preparation may wait, and a fence committed before the applicable Store barrier rejects that stage; a later fence cannot retract the committed stage. Ordinary record validation alone cannot do that. The proposed method must return Store-owned correlation rather than caller-generated sequence numbers; result/observation ordering still uses the existing receipt contract. Independent review must challenge whether this phase can instead be collapsed into a later single claim without weakening the accepted receiver or authorizing attach implicitly. No change to runtime_claim or source acceptance is assumed here.

EXEC-D1 decision: the successful Store commit of each runtime_effect_begun is that substep's irrevocable effect-authorization boundary. A fence committed before it makes the transaction reject. A fence committed after it, including before the first daemon byte, cannot establish retraction of this already committed effect; it forbids any next stage/new authorization and triggers containment. A locally observed terminal/fence/deadline still suppresses unsent bytes, but absence of a local update is not proof no durable fence occurred. There is no lock spanning SQLite and the daemon syscall, and no byte-level atomic cancellation claim. After the final fresh acknowledgement, check owning PID/thread, retained epoch/ticket, local terminal/fence state, trusted daemon/containment liveness and the original boot/time-namespace identity and absolute BOOTTIME deadline before every bounded send/poll/parse operation. Recheck after waking, including suspend-like BOOTTIME expiry while wall/MONOTONIC remain apparently valid. Do not recapture an anchor or extend a relative timeout. Serialize positive submissions and local fence delivery for deterministic in-process behavior, without claiming that this serializes every Store fence caller. Once the Store barrier has committed, any uncertainty about whether bytes were sent remains potentially effective and held, even if the first byte was delayed. Partial writes, stalls, cancellation or lost replies are UNKNOWN unless separately reconciled. Already committed late starts remain subject to guardian's original deadline and independent host containment; no exactly-once or hard-real-time claim follows.

Fixed daemon HTTP and attach boundary

Bootstrap supplies only a verified fixed local AF_UNIX/SOCK_STREAM daemon route /var/run/docker.sock and immutable identity/permission expectations. Revalidate socket inode/type/ownership, authenticated peer identity and trusted daemon/image provenance under the reviewed host model; pathname existence, a version string and root UID alone do not authenticate a daemon. Do not use DOCKER_HOST, Docker contexts, proxy/registry configuration, HTTP redirects, TCP fallback, credentials or arbitrary URLs/methods. Runtime provenance must be supported by the actual selected host before any mode is admitted.

API paths are generated from API1.51 constants plus Store name/exact64-hex container ID. Allow only the pinned image inspection, create, exact-ID inspect, non-TTY attach, start and separately authorized negative operations. No pull/build/exec/list-all/general API facility. Create body is the accepted compiler's exact bytes (at most65536). Each mutation uses a dedicated connection and at most one request, with no reconnect/resend even on a broken response. Connection setup is nonblocking; first possible request byte consumes local send permission. A timeout does not establish daemon no-effect, including a connect/write error whose effect boundary is uncertain.

Proposed finite transport limits, not a live allowance: header16384bytes,64fields,2048bytes per line; JSON body1048576bytes maximum (create acknowledgement4096); read/write chunk32768bytes; at most64 bounded parser/I/O steps per scheduler cycle with clock checks between calls; poll quantum at most100ms and per-operation ceiling4seconds, always clipped by the original anchor. No background blocking reader, unbounded queue or reset-on-progress timeout. Request/response inventories remain within Store's existing32 action/claim bound; a create subprotocol permits at most one pinned-image inspect and one create request; a start subprotocol permits exactly two pre-start exact-ID inspections, one attach handshake and one start request, each at most once. A failed/unused substep does not allow a replacement. These internal steps belong to that same claimed submission and do not mint general inspection/attach permission. These implementation ceilings may only be narrowed by the eventual frozen candidate; changing an approved experiment's limits requires new authority.

Use a bounded strict HTTP/1.1 codec: reject malformed/duplicate authority-bearing headers, control characters, conflicting length/transfer encoding, unsupported compression, interim responses, redirects, excess/truncated bodies and trailing response data. Support exactly one explicitly decoded Content-Length body or strict bounded chunked body; chunk metadata/trailers and total wire bytes need separate finite caps in the implementation plan. JSON rejects duplicate keys, invalid UTF-8 and excessive structures; reuse recipe aggregate/type bounds before inspection. Status/body schema and warnings are validated per fixed operation. No raw daemon errors/configuration are copied into public reports. Unknown or weakening create warnings fail closed; preserve any returned exact ID for negative reconciliation rather than reporting no effect.

Attach is an explicitly declared permission-bearing substep of the already claimed start submission. EXEC-D2 decision: obtain a fresh successful runtime_effect_begin(request, attach) acknowledgement before any attach request byte; it revalidates current original clock/epoch/policy/positive authority and consumes exactly one attach attempt. Open only the confirmed exact created container, stdin/stdout/stderr selected, stream=true, logs=false, non-TTY, and send no app input before start authority. The exact HTTP upgrade request/response allowlist must be checked against pinned official daemon source before codec implementation. This proposal chooses an authenticated101 upgrade to the raw stream, with no silent200/alternate-mode fallback; physical compatibility remains unqualified. Coalesced post-header bytes enter Docker Output immediately, never a JSON body decoder. Only the one bound attach connection carries guardian input/output; no reconnect, log replay or second writer. The handshake has the proposed4second ceiling clipped to the original anchor; an established stream lives only within that anchor and its finite cumulative byte/frame/queue bounds, not a renewed4second run. No app input is allowed until a separately acknowledged start stage and qualified startup admission. Attach loss/EOF or validation failure is terminal: close both directions, fence, prohibit start and preserve UNKNOWN/held capacity. Success followed by rejection/loss of the start-stage acknowledgement also closes/fences without automatic NoEffectResolution, LaunchClosure or release. An attached socket is neither restart state nor authority.

Create, start and stream composition

  1. Bootstrap pins/seals sources and establishes trusted daemon and independent containment before positive admission. Store reserves/prepares/dispatches as already specified; this supplies no physical effect. The create receiver obtains its one fresh claim, validates the pinned image and exact recipe, obtains fresh Store effect-begin(create) acknowledgement and performs the bounded create. Accept only one full exact container ID with fixed ticket name/labels and qualified response; no name-only success. Persist correlated SubmissionObserved and OperationReceipt through the existing client path. Failure to acknowledge persistence keeps the effect uncertain and forbids start.
  2. Start requires its own new runtime_action/runtime_claim and the one protected confirmed create receipt. Retrieve the original create envelope and pinned image only as inspection expectations; verify_created takes that create envelope because it deliberately rejects a start request. Compare start target with the exact created ID and fresh never-started inspect. After the fresh never-started inspection, obtain the attach-stage Store barrier and open exactly one bounded attach connection. Retain its authenticated live handshake only in the owning receiver's current pending state. Perform one more exact-ID never-started inspection, then obtain the start-stage Store barrier and recheck clock immediately before exact-ID start. The receiver requires that same live attach-ready state before asking Store for start-stage permission and again before its send; no public caller may construct it from bytes or disk. A returned Store marker without this current pending state is not send permission. A prior create request, inspect success or attached socket never substitutes for fresh start authority.
  3. Demultiplex only Docker stdout stream1 with zero reserved bytes using accepted Output. Forward each returned payload in chunks at most32768bytes into the accepted guardian Parser and drain complete inner frames between chunks. Check the original deadline between every bounded call; if the per-cycle step cap is reached, retain at most one remaining outer payload (65536bytes) and resume draining before accepting more outer bytes. Distinguish empty b'' from incomplete None. Both parser pending limits remain131072; outer total/frame caps and guardian output quota stay unchanged. Account for this residual payload and finite downstream input/output queues explicitly in the transport memory budget; full queues suspend admission of bytes, never clocks/containment.
  4. Guardian S frames carry only ARMED then CHILD_RELEASED. Inner O/E bytes remain app output and cannot create lifecycle status. Application stdin is encoded only as bounded guardian I frames, with a finite cumulative allowance from the reviewed candidate; none is supplied by this proposal. Parser/quota/EOF/clock failure terminally closes the whole composed transport and fences; complete both parsers' drained EOF checks. Neither clean EOF nor guardian status proves exec, configuration, absence or capacity release.

Sealing, authenticated startup and containment

Seven declared file hashes and read-only bind flags are insufficient. Bootstrap must prove immutable bytes and daemon-visible inode/path/mount identity for the whole claim-to-exec interval, including ancestor replacement, symlink/hardlink/physical aliases and same-UID mutation. Retained read descriptors alone do not pin a daemon path lookup. No chmod-only or readonly-in-container assertion qualifies host sealing. This contract does not select an unproven sealing mechanism: a separately reviewed supported host mechanism and its exact source/layout bindings are prerequisites, and changing policy source paths requires a fresh Store-bound policy before reservation.

Trusted host observation must bind boot ID, pid/start ticks/pidfd, namespace and credential facts, guardian PID1, actual app executable/argv/environment/FD allowlist, actual mounts and inherited exact Store time namespace to the same ticket/instance/start submission and five-file managed manifest. Then use the accepted process-bound startup evidence checks through Store. Stated image Env removals are directives, not process environment observations. Guardian release is not successful exec. Process/config evidence without an authenticated producer cannot admit a runtime. Provider mediation additionally requires an authenticated bounded route with credentials held outside app/project access and no bypass; the historical tokenless fixture endpoint does not qualify it. A3 additionally requires native assigning-session/child lineage before any delegated mode.

Independent containment must be ready and acknowledged before the first positive effect, survive management/Store/output stalls, retain exact intended name/labels plus discovered immutable IDs, and use only its separately reviewed finite stop/remove/observe scope and deadlines. It cannot renew launch authority, use expired Store action calls after the deadline, or mint release evidence by fiat. A shared daemon may still complete an in-flight create after a final absence observation; repeated name absence is not proof of launch closure. If the host cannot prove no future materialization within the fixed cleanup window, retain UNKNOWN/held capacity and report incomplete containment. Do not widen deadlines or substitute broad host cleanup. Host/kernel/scheduler failure remains outside a hard-real-time guarantee.

Protected PID1 guardian · accepted implementation contract

Scope/order: Main owns a small native Linux guardian source and host protocol parser; the existing reviewer and QA retain independent gates. Do not reinterpret the existing app argv as a wrapper or dispatch any effect using a standalone guardian plan. No selected new daemon/runtime architecture.

Executable boundary: guardian remains private namespace PID1 and refuses other PID, nonpositive UID/GID, nonzero permitted/effective/inheritable/ambient capabilities, absent no-new-privileges, changed boot UUID/time namespace identity, expired original boottime deadline, or malformed/extra arguments. Fixed production child argv is /rnr/codex app-server --listen stdio://. Guardian path /rnr/guardian; neither path nor ancestors may be covered by writable mounts. Expected boot UUID, time-ns device/inode, captured boottime and absolute deadline are exact decimal/UUID arguments derived solely from the retained Store anchor, not durations. Each numeric field is positive signed64-bit, current boottime must not precede captured time. No environment-driven behavior, PATH lookup, shell, dynamic code/config load or project override. Child environment is an explicit fixed minimal whitelist for HOME/CODEX_HOME and locale; native child uses HOME/CODEX_HOME=/rnr/home, LANG=C.UTF-8 and cwd/; the supervised policy must bind these exact paths before integration. Privately built static native binary preferred; compiler/headers may be downloaded/extracted into project-private state with hashes/provenance, never installed globally. Build and runtime image acceptance remain separate.

Lifecycle: validate identity, clear dumpability and read back PR_GET_DUMPABLE=0 before any fork; preserve it by never execing or changing guardian credentials. Require host stdin/stdout FIFO or SOCK_STREAM, then set nonblocking; ignore SIGPIPE. Arm CLOCK_BOOTTIME timerfd with TFD_TIMER_ABSTIME and verify retained absolute deadline before child release. Child receives only dedicated pipe fds0/1/2; close all other descriptors, including timerfd and raw guardian host output. Use a one-byte gate pipe released only after timer arming and an immediate deadline check; child closes that gate and rechecks deadline immediately before fixed execve. Parent status means ARMED or CHILD_RELEASED, never proof of successful app exec. A child CLOEXEC-pipe EOF cannot prove exec, so do not turn it into lifecycle authority. Actual application process observation remains required. On startup failure, app exit, expired timer, host stdin EOF, malformed input, I/O error or buffer exhaustion: exit PID1 immediately without flush/drain/fsync/callback/retry. This kills remaining namespace descendants subject to kernel/scheduler limitations; host authoritative observation still controls release.

Framing: raw guardian stdout contains frames with one ASCII type byte plus a four-byte network-order length and at most65536 payload bytes. O/E frames carry child stdout/stderr; S frames carry only fixed guardian-generated status (ARMED then CHILD_RELEASED, once each). Child never receives that raw descriptor; its bytes are always payload inside O/E. Host stdin accepts only I frames carrying app input, including zero-length harmless input; unknown types/oversize/truncated frame at EOF terminate. No deadline update, command selection or lifecycle authority can arrive through stdin. Status failure diagnostics are best effort only; terminal exit never waits to send them. Host parser has the same closed schema and finite aggregate input/output buffers; forged S bytes inside O remain application bytes.

Finite relay: at most128KiB pending host-output and128KiB pending child-input, frame payload65536 and fixed bounded reads/writes per poll iteration. Check time and prioritize timer before other events every iteration. Poll timeout derives from original absolute deadline and cannot exceed100ms. One bounded child-stdout and child-stderr read per iteration; overflow terminates instead of blocking. Aggregate application output is bounded by the supervised policy quota (hard implementation ceiling16MiB), counted before queueing; guardian status overhead has a separate fixed small bound. Reap the direct child with WNOHANG every iteration; app exit terminates namespace even with live descendants. No synchronous file writes or logs on terminal path. Cgroup/namespace restrictions, process quota including guardian and independently reserved host containment are required outside this primitive; no hard-real-time claim under exhaustion, SIGSTOP, kernel stall or host freeze.

Validation: offline compile with warnings/errors and static analysis where available; host parser adversarial framing tests; bounded non-PID1 invocation rejects before any child. A separately frozen finite local container qualification later covers genuine PID1, timer armed before launch, deadline already expired, child/descendant exit, blocked host reader, input loss, malformed/truncated/oversize frames, output floods, same-UID signal/ptrace/procfd/process_vm/timerfd attacks and clock identity mismatch. Store-owned original runtime clock · independently accepted

The accepted dormant implementation adds an opt-in Linux clock binding to the sole Store, without an executor or enabled operational constructor. Existing synthetic stores keep their exact configuration and behavior. A new constructor flag runtime_clock=True is part of immutable configuration only when selected; it requires the ordinary trusted system wall clock, not a caller-selected clock callback. No runtime effects occur during clock capture. The Store captures once while creating a new journal, persists one typed RuntimeClockAnchor in the existing transitions table in the same initialization transaction, and returns it through a read-only runtime_clock operation. No second journal/schema, caller-supplied anchor, refresh or update operation.

Anchor fields: version1, run ID, supervisor epoch, configuration digest, boot UUID, time-namespace nsfs device/inode, captured boottime nanoseconds, sampled wall nanoseconds, original wall deadline nanoseconds, and absolute boottime deadline nanoseconds. Require an exact boolean mode. Read exact bounded boot ID and executing worker time namespace identity (/proc/thread-self/ns/time) before and after sampling; reject changes/unreadable values. Sample CLOCK_BOOTTIME before system wall time; convert the existing finite float run deadline conservatively using its exact integer ratio, rounded down to nanoseconds. Absolute deadline = earlier boottime sample + original wall deadline minus sampled wall time. Reject nonpositive remaining time and invalid/overflowed signed64-bit values. This ordering shortens the allowance by sampling latency. Suspend consumes the allowance because CLOCK_BOOTTIME advances through suspend. Trust in host wall-clock initialization remains the existing trust boundary; this does not establish a new run budget.

On reopening, load exactly one retained anchor, validate closed type/content and exact run/epoch/config/deadline correlation, then verify current boot and executing worker time namespace identity, anchor arithmetic and current boottime not preceding its captured value. Never recapture or replace the anchor. A different boot/time namespace fails opening conservatively; historical held allocations require separate explicit reconciliation. Same-clock expired anchors remain readable for containment but reject positive runtime action/claim. The central _positive hook checks current boot/time namespace identity and boottime on every positive admission, including runtime authorization and receiving claims, alongside existing wall rules. Existing restart fences remain unchanged. Negative containment continues under existing bounds, never frees capacity from a clock observation. A read is evidence of the retained bound, never dispatch authority.

Manual trace before tests: constructor validates mode and captures immutable configuration; worker creates or verifies one journal anchor before ready; positive authorization checks retained anchor before recording a commitment; receiving claim checks again before one-shot handoff; restart reads original anchor and existing fences rather than minting time. Before adding a real executor, close the receiver restart/lost-ack gap: authenticated bytes and a sender commitment alone do not establish that a particular receiver has not already consumed the request.

Store runtime_action additionally retains the exact immutable RuntimeDispatch envelope in its existing transition journal. New runtime_claim(request) accepts only that exact retained envelope, matching its protected authorization transition, current run/epoch/ticket/policy, original deadline and unreleased allocation. Positive create/start claims recheck current admission/generation/workspace authority and launch closure, including intervening cleanup commitments. Positive claims additionally reject any allocation receipt added after their authorization, preventing changed lifecycle evidence from being silently consumed; this conservative rejection requires fresh reconciliation, never request replay. All claims reject prior operation/observation, SubmissionObserved or no-effect resolution for that same submission. Negative containment may retain its exact previously authorized target without expanding it. A claim transaction appends runtime_action_claimed once per submission before returning its fresh Future result. Reconstructed/read envelopes and duplicate claims cannot create another claim. Restart never resumes a claimed effect; lost claim acknowledgement remains uncertain and capacity held. No new journal, reset, release or external effect is introduced.

RuntimeDispatchReceiver consumes one exact accepted Channel and the owning Store, on one process/thread, with a bounded original wall-to-monotonic ceiling and at most32 requests for one ticket. It binds its ticket/key from Store before decoding any packet, then decodes the closed version/run/epoch/dispatch packet, requests runtime_claim, waits within the existing deadline and returns only the freshly committed request to its trusted effect caller. A fresh successful claim completion is a one-shot handoff, never an exactly-once external-effect guarantee. No callback/future continuation executes late results. One pending claim at a time; a second receive before response is rejected. respond(result) verifies exact typed operation/observation, submission/action/sequence/instance correlation and emits the client's existing response envelope. Any uncertain failure permanently closes the receiver/channel and requests generation fencing with the previously bound ticket. No resend or completion after expiry; response loss leaves the claim consumed.

Bootstrap must colocate this receiver with the sole Store authority or supply a separately qualified protected Store transport; the implementation does not share a Store object across fork or create a second SQLite owner. Existing client/local IPC tests can colocate trusted endpoints; that proves no process isolation. Real executor, independent deadline supervision, process separation, authenticated provider mediation and operational construction remain disabled/pending. This stage adds no injectable real effect callbacks or shell commands. Tests must cover fabricated/changed envelopes, duplicate/concurrent claims, claim before response, lost acknowledgement before/after commit, restart, fences/cleanup between authorization and claim, malformed correlation and held capacity. Reuse unchanged accepted tests; run only affected Store/bridge and new receiver checks.

Main added a protected Store dispatch operation and a bounded client bridge. Store validates the exact ticket/epoch/action and retained launch policy, current generation/workspace/turn authority for create/start, original deadline, unreleased dispatching allocation, one create/start commitment per action and no prior launch closure. Start additionally requires an exact persisted confirmed create result. Negative inspect/stop/remove remains available for containment after admission fencing, within original bounds and exact instance identity. Every accepted commitment is persisted before a packet can be queued; the read/observation receipt API is not authorization. Repeated authorization, even with identical content, cannot resend. Recovery never automatically retransmits.

The bridge obtains policy/target from Store, not request payload overrides, and sends only a closed typed envelope on the accepted process channel. Connection identity supplements existing run/epoch/ticket/submission correlation, never replaces it. Pre-code decisions after independent review: first implementation is runtime-only; workload dispatch remains deferred and rejected by this API. New Store runtime_action(ticket_id, action) atomically validates retained authority, generates submission and sequence, persists DispatchCommit plus a protected authorization transition, and returns an immutable RuntimeDispatch containing commit, retained ticket/policy, exact target and transition. No caller-supplied commit/sequence/policy/target/deadline. Sequence exceeds both the journal transition maximum and all saved receipt/closure sequences for this ticket; finite sequence/inventory limits fail closed. Submission hashes run, supervisor epoch, ticket, action and sequence. Receipt insertion alone never creates the protected authorization transition. Create/start each permit one commitment; containment uses fresh bounded submissions, never retries an old one.

A runtime client binds one Store-authoritative ticket/key and one already bootstrap-bound Channel to its owning process/Thread. Only successful completion of its own fresh runtime_action request can send once; reads, deserialized records, reconnects and late Future completions cannot send. Store timeout or transport failure closes the client/channel first, then requests generation fencing with that previously validated key; a failed fence remains unknown, never release. The transaction linearizes commitment: earlier fences reject positives, later fences retain possibly late effects. No Store/wire atomicity claim. Original wall-deadline is conservatively anchored to monotonic time; each call also has the channel's30-second maximum.

Start requires prepared process-bound startup, an exact confirmed materialized create receipt linked to a protected current-epoch create commitment, and no contradictory instance/outcome evidence. A matching no-effect create resolution or protected confirmed absence blocks start. Any stop/remove commitment permanently closes further positives even if cleanup acknowledgement is lost. Known exact instances remain available for containment. Read-only inspection may legitimately report effect=none together with present state. Inspect may use fixed ticket allocation/name/labels while instance is unknown. Stop/remove require a confirmed exact instance from protected create or inspect evidence; conflicting identities reject rather than select one. Closed responses must match run/epoch/commit digest, submission/action/ticket and target before existing receipt persistence. Authenticated peer bytes alone are not lifecycle proof: the future receiver/evidence producer remains unqualified and constructors disabled. Restart preserves original epoch/configuration and fences inventory; historical reconciliation may add evidence, never create a new send capability or bypass existing release requirements.

D00 protected process channel: source and local IPC accepted

Implementation plan, 16 November 2026; baseline b6046fc. Same RNR-D00, issue/counter UNKNOWN. Main is sole source/Git writer; existing reviewer owns source acceptance and existing QA owns independent checks. This prerequisite adds no operational launcher or alternate accounting authority. Existing Controller, BootstrapAdapter and Supervisor stay disabled. Store retains admission, dispatch commitment, epoch fencing and release authority.

Implement automation/control_runtime_channel.py as Linux-only bounded packet transport between trusted management processes. Trusted bootstrap creates an unnamed AF_UNIX/SOCK_SEQPACKET pair, enables SO_PASSCRED on both before transfer, and hands each endpoint exclusively to its intended process. No filesystem listener, discovery, project-container mount or token. SO_PASSCRED may kernel-autobind an abstract name after sending; name emptiness is not an authentication invariant. Trusted bootstrap supplies socketpair provenance; connected-socket checks do not prove it. Bootstrap must bind expected PID, real UID/GID, boot ID and start ticks from its own spawn evidence; caller-supplied packet identities never establish trust. Bootstrap supplies an owned pidfd acquired at creation or while it guarantees the child cannot be reaped; identity reads alone do not prevent PID reuse. Channel consumes that handle, verifies its fdinfo PID and observed process identity, and checks liveness. Check pidfd before and after each packet. Keep owner PID to reject inherited wrapper use. Each receive requires exactly one kernel SCM_CREDENTIALS matching the expected peer; reject ancillary descriptors, truncation, oversized/empty packets, stale peers and expired deadlines, closing received descriptors even on malformed messages.

Use at most65536 bytes per packet, monotonic deadlines and nonblocking I/O. An operation failure permanently closes this endpoint; no automatic resend/reconnect. Successful send means queued bytes only, never execution acknowledgement or release. Any later RPC integration must bind Store epoch/ticket/operation and persist commitments before effects; restart or lost acknowledgement retains existing uncertainty until authoritative reconciliation. The transport does not decode or authorize commands and cannot mint receipts.

Security prerequisites: trusted bootstrap and management code; management peers must not exec or change credentials/namespaces during channel lifetime. SCM_CREDENTIALS contains kernel-validated sender-specified or default IDs; explicit IDs can match real/effective/saved IDs and privileged senders have exceptions. Stable credentials are a bootstrap prerequisite, not a packet-level real-ID attestation; no adversarial CAP_SYS_ADMIN credential forgery, ptrace, proc-fd access or descriptor injection into the trusted peer; privileged-host compromise is outside this primitive. Project execution must separately be isolated from management processes/provider credentials and inherited endpoints. Process credentials establish sender identity under these prerequisites, not executable integrity or provider isolation. Avoid claiming A2/A3 closure from this primitive.

  1. Review this contract against Store/supervisor interfaces and Linux credential semantics.
  2. Write focused failing tests, then implement bounded packet/identity handling. Trace bootstrap → peer binding → send/receive → rejection/close; cover wrong sender, inherited wrapper, PID identity mismatch, dead peer, timeout, oversized packet and descriptor leak rejection.
  3. Main and the same QA may execute narrowly bounded local IPC unit tests: one child at a time, no shells/container/model/provider/network listeners/credentials, explicit timeout and reap. This extends QA's prior offline-only assignment solely for these tests; it is not broad independent operational QA.
  4. Obtain same-reviewer source acceptance and separate QA, publish, then integrate a real supervised adapter through Store in a subsequent qualified step. Do not enable operational execution on primitive acceptance.

Primary references: Linux unix(7) (per-message credentials and descriptor disposal) and pidfd_open(2) (process lifetime). SO_PEERCRED at socket creation is insufficient for inherited endpoints; pollable pidfds detect exit without trusting a reused numeric PID.

Supported admission and lifecycle boundaries · revision 30

Result: pinned Core provides a stronger public local Rust tree-shutdown handle, but supported access and complete observed descendant/container release remain unproven for the proposed Python/app-server runner. The named turn-start admission hook is a shutdown-drain control, not by itself universal budget admission. This source-only D00 investigation narrows the missing evidence; it does not enable execution or choose a new Rust topology. All sources below are official openai/codex tag rust-v0.167.0. Upstream tests were read, not run.

Inspected controlEstablished meaning and remaining boundary
Turn-start admissionTurnStartAdmission retains a host permit through submission for shutdown draining. Existing-turn steering, memory-only mailbox wakeups and parent-delegated input bypass this hook. ThreadSpawn input requires parent-turn metadata for that bypass; explicit Review delegate input has a separate exception. The app-server implementation counts active permits while not closed/paused; it contains no budget/capacity reservation. Client RPCs have separate host gating. These scoped observations do not prove an admission bypass across all controls or authenticate caller-supplied lineage. This hook alone cannot satisfy the runner's global pre-turn reservation contract.
Public Rust tree shutdownThreadManager::request_agent_tree_shutdown fences starts for the exact local runtime and returns AgentTreeShutdown. Its wait/wait_detailed methods wait for admitted operations/sessions and report tracked cleanup or persistence failures. Registry removal and caller-owned persistence handoff remain with the caller; top-level loads/resumes require separate serialization. This is stronger than thread interruption. No supported mapping to the chosen Python/app-server interface was established, and completion is not proof of all OS descendants or containers being absent.
Session and background-process cleanupSession shutdown invokes unified-exec cleanup and other lifecycle handlers. Bulk cleanup drains entries and calls terminate rather than awaiting confirmed termination for each process; the remote path dispatches asynchronous termination. The separate background-terminal handler targets one tracked process, not a whole allocation. Local PTY termination is best effort and does not wait for OS reaping. Preserve the earlier measured single-command-tree stop, without generalizing it to universal release.
Command-session terminationcommand/exec/terminate handling acknowledges request_terminate before the separate exit signal. The process ID is scoped to the original client connection. An empty RPC response is not observed process exit or allocation release.
Unsubscribe and archiveUnsubscribe removes this connection's subscription. The archive preparation helper proceeds even if Shutdown submission fails or times out; archiving the stored subtree does not repair that missing confirmation. Neither operation substitutes for a release receipt.
Saved plugin listThreadStartResponse.disabled_plugin_ids is documented as a saved list that does not yet filter plugin capabilities. This field is not established isolation enforcement; the comment does not prove every plugin remains usable or negate separate configuration, authorization or environment restrictions.

Required next evidence: establish a supported control surface for the chosen topology; enumerate owned runtime/process/container allocations and all admission paths; bind admission and lineage to authenticated assignments; prevent concurrent reload/start during release; and independently observe process exit and container deletion before releasing capacity. Failed or unknown termination stays unreleased and requires reconciliation. Static source inspection cannot supply those runtime observations. Internal extension ToolPolicy/SessionIsolation types do not become Python configuration fields merely because their Rust source is public.

Scope and review: dedicated native owner v5 maintains this existing plan/handoff; independent nonauthor /root/advance_runner_next_slice/review_supported_lifecycle, canonical development-standards-rnr-d00-lifecycle-reviewer, traced the lifecycle controls and independently challenged the admission conclusions. No SDK/client/app-server, model call, container or runtime probe was launched. A1/A2/A3, callback wire validation, R19-F01, all full TW scenarios and descendant release remain open. Both live allowances remain expired. No configuration, topology, security setting, credential, budget or runtime source change is made.

Stop-adapter consequence: a successful turn/interrupt is not a process-stop receipt. Track owned terminal/process IDs, explicitly terminate active commands, verify descendants stopped, and retain the allocation until runtime/container shutdown is confirmed. On missing or failed terminal termination, stop the owned container and reconcile before resume/transfer. The pinned SDK did not export the observed terminal response type; the probe used its generic typed request with the runtime schema. Production compatibility and failure handling must be implemented and tested in D02; no private SDK process field from this diagnostic becomes a production contract.

Concrete contracts for the first implementation

These are proposed implementation decisions within the existing single-application design. Keep the six component boundaries. Prefer small public interfaces over many shallow modules; split files only as behavior warrants. Keep SDK types inside the runtime adapter. Use Python data structures and versioned JSON at boundaries, SQLite for durable facts and an ordinary command loop; no new coordinator service, queue server or agent framework.

ContractImplementation decision and rejection behavior
Versioned recordsThe existing TaskPacket, AgentResult, VerificationReport and OperationReceipt use schema_version=1, opaque IDs, positive ownership/plan versions, UTC timestamps and content hashes. Validate required fields and enums before use. Reject unsupported schema versions and malformed authority fields; never silently drop a restriction. Keep evidence as bounded references plus hashes, not secret-bearing raw transcripts.
Authority and caller identityResolve the effective authority from the saved human approval, trusted base configuration and registered transport/session. Match the caller's run, role, assignment and ownership version before any control operation. Caller-supplied parent names, approval booleans, paths or commands cannot grant access. Keep separate read-only snapshots of untrusted issue/agent content; pass external content using supported lower-trust messages.
Runtime adapterUse probe() → CapabilityReport, dispatch(packet) → OperationReceipt, inspect(assignment) → RuntimeSnapshot, send(message) → OperationReceipt, stop(assignment) → OperationReceipt and release(assignment) → OperationReceipt. Dispatch identifies new or resumed work and its stable request ID. A capability report names exact SDK/runtime versions, mode, checked evidence and limitations. An inspection distinguishes unknown, active, stopped and released; neither a final response nor successful interrupt submission proves release.
Messages and outcomesA message carries a stable ID, source event ID, registered sender, intended owner/version, supervisor, kind and referenced finding. Deduplicate before delivery. Ordinary feedback is delivered at a verified checkpoint; urgent stop is a separate authorized control action. AgentResult can propose an outcome but cannot advance acceptance or authority. Record rejected stale results for reconciliation.
Transactions and effectsOne controller process holds the host/repository lock. SQLite transactions atomically claim an issue, reserve allocations/resources, advance the ownership version and save the operation intent. No network call is held inside a transaction. Operation state is pending → in_flight → confirmed | failed | unknown; unknown requires read-back before retry. A unique request ID identifies one logical action; intentionally new delivery attempts receive new IDs.
Execution versus external stateKeep the existing teamwork gate, run hold/cancellation, GitHub issue/PR state, acceptance evidence and finalization status as separate fields. Validate allowed gate transitions against the evidence, not an agent's requested status. Accepted closure is historical; later work gets a linked issue. Premature closure returns to the original unfinished acceptance. Independent work can proceed using confirmed-free resources.
Accounting and recoveryRecord provider events with a unique source/session/turn/event key. Distinguish cumulative snapshots from deltas to avoid counting replayed usage twice. Missing usage is unknown, never zero. Preserve original deadlines, spent allowance and reservations across restart, hold, correction and ownership transfer. A lost dispatch response is reconciled against its session/attempt before another turn. If observation is insufficient, block the dependent action.
Store compatibilityKeep the database and evidence in a private local directory outside agent mounts. Use schema-versioned transactional migrations, verified backups and the existing post-backup reconciliation rule. Do not use a network filesystem or unsupported cross-host failover. Refuse an unknown newer schema without mutation; rollback must not discard later receipts, limits or ownership history.
Source, checks and host GitA candidate reference identifies base revision plus exact file content, modes, symlink targets and relevant untracked files. Freeze only after writers stop. Check evidence names that candidate and the trusted check definition. The host Git adapter uses controller-owned configuration and an allowlisted environment, rejects candidate configuration/hooks/executable filters and never runs project checks. Exercise hostile hooks/attributes/configuration using synthetic fixtures before enabling host publication.

The pinned SDK's convenience thread_start defaults to automatic approval review, and its low-level default command/file approval handler accepts requests. Neither is the runner's authority policy. Set the required approval/sandbox values explicitly, reject unexpected approval requests, and verify returned effective settings. Use the supported typed low-level API when dynamic tools are needed; do not build production control on private SDK fields used only for probe diagnostics. Bound SDK calls by the saved controller deadline; a timeout leaves its effect unknown until reconciled rather than authorizing another dispatch.

Keep versioned shared contracts in proposed automation/contracts.py; settings and eligibility in automation/config.py and automation/selection.py; private state/effects in automation/store.py; SDK, GitHub and workspace boundaries in automation/runtime.py, automation/github.py and automation/workspace.py; orchestration and reporting in automation/controller.py and automation/cli.py. These paths are proposed, not existing code or instructions to scaffold everything now. The selected issue owns only the files it needs. One integrator owns shared contracts, bootstrap.py and any shared test fixtures; other independent work proceeds against their reviewed version. Tests follow the existing unittest convention under tests/.

RNR-D01 maintenance: strict JSON bytes and finite numbers

Stage and scope: source-only maintenance of the existing D01 input contract, using automation/contracts.py::load_json. No new public issue, runtime component or parallel plan is created. The shared loader supplies enrollment, immutable configuration, offline snapshots, injected GitHub responses and canonical journal reads.

Established cause and chosen correction: direct json.loads(bytes) auto-detects UTF-16/32, and parse_constant does not see numeric exponents that overflow to infinity. Reproduction accepted all six UTF-16/32 encodings plus positive/negative overflow despite the loader's UTF-8 and nonfinite errors. Decode bytes/bytearray as UTF-8 explicitly and reject nonfinite parse_float results. Preserve a leading UTF-8 byte-order mark, original raw-byte limits, existing string-input behavior, finite floating-point semantics and exact integer parsing. No decimal conversion, recursion policy or Unicode normalization is added.

RNR-D01 maintenance: consistent requirement-text validation

Established defect: JSON may escape a lone Unicode surrogate even when its bytes are valid UTF-8. The snapshot parser previously accepted that title/body; preview reported approval_missing without an approval but failed UTF-8 scope hashing when one existed. Excluded issues also escaped the hash path, and the injected adapter could read dependency endpoints before discovering any encoding problem. CLI exception handling was already safe; this is inconsistent source validation, not execution or approval bypass.

Bounded contract: after existing title/body type and length checks in automation/selection.py::parse_snapshot, require both fields to encode as strict UTF-8. Catch only UnicodeEncodeError and raise the fixed ValueError issue title and body must be UTF-8 encodable. Validate every issue before approval/status/label filtering and before the adapter's dependency reads. Preserve valid non-ASCII and astral text, JSON-decoded valid surrogate pairs, empty bodies and existing scope digests. Do not change generic JSON/string validation, normalize or replace text, or add a recursion policy.

Approaches and decision: changing all JSON consumers would broaden the accepted-input contract unnecessarily; changing scope hashing to escape or replace text would change approval identities. The selected narrow parser check makes already hash-bound requirement text consistently valid before selection. Source-only disabled-runtime callback

Spec and goal: implement the explicit rejection boundary required by the existing SDK adapter contract while runtime execution remains disabled. The pinned SDK default accepts command/file approval requests; that is not the runner policy. This preparation adds no runtime construction, SDK import, command, file/network operation or CLI integration.

Supported-path finding: AsyncCodexClient is a thread-offloading wrapper around the same synchronous client and exposes no public callback constructor/deferred-response control. Switching to it does not resolve A1. Wrapper construction; request forwarding. The synchronous client exposes a public approval_handler constructor argument, but invokes it on the reader thread. This source work provides only a fast refusal function for possible future explicit injection. It does not substitute a private-method override, extra queue service or polling protocol.

Interface: automation/callbacks.py::reject_server_request(method: str, params: dict | None) -> dict. Require an exact string method and exact dictionary or None parameters. Do not traverse, log, echo, normalize or derive authority from request payloads. Exact item/commandExecution/requestApproval and item/fileChange/requestApproval return {"decision":"cancel"}. Exact item/tool/call returns {"success":false,"contentItems":[{"type":"inputText","text":"Runner execution is disabled."}]}. Every call returns fresh response objects. Every other or malformed request raises UnsupportedServerRequest(ValueError) with a fixed bounded message that excludes request data. There is no success/accept/default-empty fallback.

Protocol basis: pin method names and response shapes to ServerRequest, command decisions, file decisions and dynamic responses. This is an intentionally restricted refusal handler, not a complete implementation of every server request or a runtime capability claim. Unknown permission, user-input, authentication, attestation, MCP and legacy approval requests are unsupported, not silently accepted.

Failure and lifecycle limits: the pinned SDK bypasses this callback for non-string wire methods, returning an empty object, and normalizes non-dict parameters to None. Direct helper type checks therefore do not establish wire validation. This unresolved integration limit follows the pinned dispatch path. A callback exception propagates to the pinned SDK reader's fail-all path; it does not prove app-server, command, descendant or container termination. An eventual authorized adapter must stop/reconcile through independently tested lifecycle controls. A cancel response is a protocol request, not evidence of released capacity. The default accepts only if an eventual caller omits the explicit handler; this source task does not claim to have changed any running SDK. A1/A2/R19-F01 and lineage/lifecycle gates stay open.

  1. Obtain independent challenge of the exact source-only refusal contract and pinned protocol evidence. Sole native owner v3 writes the two new files and existing guide/plan/handoff; reviewer owns no writes.
  2. Trace entry -> exact type/method match -> constant cancellation/failure or static exception. Write failing tests in tests/test_automation_callbacks.py for both cancellations, dynamic refusal, spoofed/secret-bearing payloads, all unsupported routes, malformed inputs, fresh response objects and JSON output. Confirm the absent module fails.
  3. Implement only the pure public function and exception in automation/callbacks.py, with no dependency, SDK/client handle, transport call, task creation or code path that can wait for that reader.
  4. Run focused callback tests then the full unittest suite; freeze for independent source review/QA. Update this existing guide/plan/handoff, stage-check, commit/push the verified change and compare exact local/private GitHub main. No live allowance is consumed.

Status: implemented after independent contract challenge; all nine focused tests and the full 199-test cloud suite pass, with one optional git-cliff skip. Independent code review/QA passed all nine tests plus 36 adversarial direct-helper cases and confirmed no SDK imports/calls or CLI integration. The identified documentation mismatch was corrected in this section. No blocking source finding remains; no runtime acceptance is implied.

D00 local Docker canary harness · 14 November 2026

Scope and authority: the user requested implementation of the remaining runner work and explicitly selected local WSL2 Docker for disposable, credential-free isolation and cleanup tests. This is a bounded implementation of CP-1/2 and part of CP-4, not a runtime adapter or a new execution mode. Preserve the cloud design and historical evidence. Both live SDK allowances remain expired; no model, authentication profile, network access, installation, image pull, existing-container mutation or production runner dispatch is included.

Approach: retain the existing container boundary and use the available local Docker daemon. A standalone standard-library operator tool, tools/runner_container_preflight.py, exercises actual containers; ordinary automation commands do not import it. Do not implement a substitute supervisor, build an image, or credit source fixture booleans as runtime proof. Use an existing Linux Python image addressed by its complete immutable sha256: ID, verified before create with pulling disabled. Explicitly select a local Unix Docker socket and a resolved Docker executable. Supply a minimal clean subprocess environment and private empty Docker configuration; never inspect or emit the operator's environment or credentials.

Interface: the operator supplies --image sha256:… --socket /var/run/docker.sock --evidence-dir /absolute/new/private/path. Refuse an existing evidence directory or symlink and use mode 0700/0600 for owned directories/files. Reports retain source/image/executable hashes, daemon version/cgroup evidence, fixed requested policy, command receipts capped at 64 KiB per stream with no shell execution, container identities, observed policy, canary assertions, cleanup evidence, documentary original UTC start/deadline, shared same-host monotonic start/cleanup/deadline for enforcement, and explicit PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED/UNKNOWN results. Always report execution_enabled=false and runtime_mode_accepted=false. Exit zero only if every implemented canary and cleanup passed; failed or unknown cleanup remains explicitly unresolved. Evidence is local operator-controlled observation, not authenticated admission or a production recovery ledger.

Container policy: one role at a time, unique generated names and ownership labels, nonroot UID/GID, read-only root, all capabilities dropped, no-new-privileges, no network, no supervisor socket/profile/home mount, no restart policy, 0.5 CPU, 128 MiB memory with swap capped to the same value, 32 PIDs, and 16 MiB tmpfs scratch. Mount only synthetic assigned source (owner writable/reviewer read-only) and synthetic Git metadata read-only. A second synthetic allocation/private controller canary remains outside all mounts. Inspect the exact image, identity, mounts and configured policy before start. Inside each role observe cgroup-v2 CPU/memory/PID limits and bounded scratch, deny forbidden writes/paths and verify assigned access; fail closed on an unsupported cgroup layout. The probe does not stress the host into OOM or exhaust PIDs.

Lifecycle and watchdog: persist generated identity before create, and start an independent watchdog process before allocating. It runs in a separate session and uses bounded Docker supervisor calls to attempt cleanup of exact matching owned resources within a reserved final 30-second cleanup window of the unchanged absolute deadline (at most 120 seconds), even if the probe is stuck or dies. Supervisor unavailability or an unconfirmed effect remains UNKNOWN; watchdog exit or elapsed time never proves cleanup. Before create, recheck the original deadline and that cleanup has not begun. Before stop/remove, inspect and validate the exact generated name, complete ownership labels and immutable container ID; changed/reused identities are never mutated. Never use a name prefix or broad prune/kill operation. Confirm watchdog continuity through allocation operations and successful final exit; a readiness handshake alone is insufficient. Creation acknowledgement has a 10-second budget; other Docker commands retain 4-second budgets, all clipped to the unchanged monotonic run deadline. These budgets are operational choices, not measured daemon latency or guaranteed success. Every Docker CLI invocation is bounded, including termination of its owned process group after a timeout even when the immediate CLI process already exited. Malformed UTF-8 is an uncertain reply, not valid JSON after byte deletion; timeout/transport loss is UNKNOWN until identity-scoped inspect reconciles the effect. Missing inspect acknowledgement is not absence. A timed-out create may materialize after a not-found observation; retain that allocation as UNKNOWN regardless of transient absence, prohibit PASS while any create/start/stop/remove operation is uncertain, and retain exact recovery identifiers for later reconciliation. Watchdog observation after a timeout is cleanup assistance, not proof that the in-flight create was fenced. An independent watchdog is only one timeout safeguard; actual quotas and deletion are enforced/observed through Docker. Record child/grandchild process identities inside one synthetic workload, force-stop the container, observe its terminal state and no remaining processes before exact deletion, and independently verify absence through a successful daemon query. Stop acknowledgements alone never establish cleanup. This probe's serialized/no-resume topology supplies its fence; full controller restart, lost-acknowledgement and concurrent reload/admission remain unproved.

Implementation steps and ownership: main Lead owns this plan, checkpoint, operator guide and Git index; dedicated Issue Implementer /root/rnr_d00_canary_imp (canonical development-standards-rnr-d00-canary-imp, ownership v1) owns only the harness and tests/test_runner_container_preflight.py. Review/QA has no source writes or permission to launch containers. First independently challenge this contract; then implement tests before source, covering safe command construction, private fresh evidence, immutable identities/pins, role permissions, malformed supervisor replies, failed/unknown cleanup, timeout→not-found→late creation, changed/reused identity, bounded output/strict supervisor JSON and watchdog ownership checks. Evidence/control files stay outside mounted synthetic source. Run offline tests without Docker. After source review and applicable authority, run one bounded local canary experiment using the existing image. Record measured results and limitations in the checkpoint and this section, then verify all project tests and whitespace. No new branch/worktree or remote publication is required for the experiment. Consequential new SDK/controller interfaces remain outside this slice.

Measured local outcome: frozen harness SHA256 6b90fe5eb41c3833ed86e76dac4c7fda96d003178226435c5f130ecdc7107c6f and 26 focused tests received independent source/QA acceptance after DCP-R1–R4 corrections. Initial real create exceeded its 4-second acknowledgement budget: the exact created container was positively identified and removed, while the run retained UNKNOWN. Only create received a reviewed 10-second budget; other calls and total bounds stayed unchanged.
