These measured narrow observations leave execution_enabled, runtime_mode_accepted and wire_validation_accepted false; no further container is authorized by this exhausted allowance.

Acceptance: a reproducible credential-free invocation saves independently inspectable real Docker evidence for the tested policy and lifecycle; unsafe/missing evidence never returns PASS. AC-02/04/06 are advanced only at this boundary. A1/A2/A3, R19-F01, authenticated creation, full CP-4/5/6, TW-01–TW-10 and D02–D07 remain open. No gate automatically enables runtime execution.

Cloud runtime preflight and control contract · revision 34

Decision: prioritize a supported supervisor and measured isolation over adding more disconnected controller fixtures. This is a source-reviewed design for the next D00 experiment, not an implemented adapter or authorization to launch one. Keep the standard container-based boundary in the existing plan. An experimental exec-server transport is an optional command transport inside that boundary, not a replacement for externally enforced quotas, protected controller state or confirmed container teardown. Do not build a new Bubblewrap/cgroup supervisor simply to work around missing host capabilities.

Current cloud evidence, 10 November 2026: clean cloud main, origin/main and private GitHub main matched e7e2a1f76811b659ad95c39a50991021f201a078 before this document change. The selected Python 3.12.14 has no importable openai_codex. Docker and Podman are absent from PATH; their usual engine sockets, /sys/fs/cgroup and systemd system/private endpoints are absent in the visible command namespace. Bubblewrap and systemd-run executables are present. These read-only observations establish no usable quota/cleanup supervisor here, not a host-wide assertion that no service exists. The selected host reports Debian 13, Bubblewrap 0.12.0 and codex-cli 0.167.0-alpha.4; this alpha CLI is not the qualified stable SDK/runtime pair. The documented setup-pc Ubuntu workflow does not establish Debian support. Desktop Docker fixture evidence does not transfer to this executor.

Ordered gateRequired observation and failure result
CP-0 · Authority and exact targetRecord the actual executor, checkout and frozen revision, sole owner, existing approvals and original deadline. Source inspection needs no SDK allowance. An offline fixture must have separately applicable command/container authority and a bounded watchdog; zero model calls do not authorize installation, credentials, security changes or a new host. No expired allowance is reused.
CP-1 · Supervisor and resource limitsBefore any process launch, identify the supported supervisor endpoint, owned allocation IDs, immutable image/runtime pins and configured CPU, memory, PID and scratch bounds. Inspect effective limits, assigned mounts, network policy and independent stop/inspect/remove operations. Missing or unsupported enforcement is BLOCKED. CLI presence, a command returning zero, a PID namespace or a local Python timer is insufficient. The existing setup-pc guide does not provision/start Docker; do not invoke the general installer as a repair for this gap.
CP-2 · Credential-free container canariesWith CP-0/1 satisfied, reuse an existing approved image and synthetic files only. Prove writer access to its assigned source, reviewer read-only source, read-only Git metadata and bounded writable scratch; deny host home, synthetic private controller data, supervisor sockets and access to another allocation. Verify effective configured resource/network limits. Do not mount an authentication profile or copy a real secret for a negative test. These container-only checks need no SDK installation or sign-in.
CP-3 · Optional exec-server transportOnly after the outer boundary is proven, test a pinned, supported stdio transport with an explicit process sandbox request. The CLI labels the service experimental. Its PID-namespace flag is not blanket process isolation: absent process/start sandbox selects no process sandbox, so the README's minimal start example is not the isolation recipe. Require observed effective policy and denied canaries, not flag text. An unsupported combination stays BLOCKED; do not fall back to an unrestricted start.
CP-4 · Stop, lost acknowledgement and restartUse a bounded synthetic child/grandchild fixture. Fence new start/reload before requesting stop; observe owned process-tree exit and exact container absence independently. Inject a lost acknowledgement and a controller restart without repeating an uncertain start. Preserve allocation identity, history and original deadline. An acknowledgement, EOF, terminal status, SDK close or lifecycle-fixture boolean alone cannot release capacity. Failed/unknown cleanup stays occupied until supported reconciliation establishes its outcome. Test a forced timeout through the independent supervisor, not only cooperative termination.
CP-5 · Pinned SDK and callback boundaryOnly once its separate setup authority and safe outer boundary exist, qualify the exact Python SDK/runtime pair and configuration provenance. Keep authentication outside test/command containers. Start with the existing fixed-refusal handler and synthetic, zero-model wire fixtures; no callback invokes the same client, calls close(), or performs blocking I/O. Confirm malformed/unsupported wire behavior explicitly. Protocol fixtures prove only the paths they exercise, not actual model-issued callbacks, user identity, admission or native child lineage.
CP-6 · Live evidence, separately authorizedAfter the prerequisites above and independent evidence review, prepare the smallest bounded live probe with explicit remaining time/turn/capacity allowance and supported isolated sign-in. Real model-issued callback, admission and mode evidence still require that approval. Direct mode means the runner's single-owner mode, not exec-server's unrelated AWS remote-transport direct option. It is only a candidate; ordinary/complex modes remain unavailable until actual creator lineage and their separate access boundaries are proved. No gate in this table automatically enables runtime execution.

Control-thread rule: the pinned SDK reader executes callbacks synchronously before draining more responses. Its public request interface exposes no per-request timeout and waits on the response waiter without a timeout. AsyncCodexClient does not create a separate response-reader design. Use an independently supervised process and original absolute deadline for any future potentially blocking SDK operation; a timeout leaves its effect UNKNOWN and requires reconciliation, not retry. Do not reach into private SDK fields. The first probe keeps the callback side-effect-free and immediately rejecting. Even a fast handler does not bound the SDK's subsequent response write, so the independent deadline still applies. A future bounded queue may separate work from the reader, but enqueue acknowledgement is neither durable admission nor completion, and no effectful queue is selected or implemented here.

Environment rule: config.env overlays inherited environment variables; it does not remove secrets. A later supervisor must supply the SDK process's explicitly reviewed environment without printing values. Empty thread environments are conditional on turn overrides; maintain the unresolved tool, hook, plugin, MCP, extension and start/resume/fork override matrix. The no-environment topology is a material design choice still requiring evidence, not a permission workaround.

Exec-server environment boundary: its Rust stdio startup overlays inherited variables and removes only a fixed restricted set. This differs from an individual process request without envPolicy, which uses supplied variables with ambient environment cleared at launch. Neither observation proves inaccessible credential/configuration files, harmless shell startup files, or absence of inherited privileged access. Preserve all three boundaries rather than calling them one sanitized environment.

Exec-server lifecycle boundary: stdio serves one nonresumable connection and shuts down its processor after disconnect. Backend cleanup drains tracked entries and calls termination without per-process observed-exit waits. The Rust supervisor waits for its direct executor child and escalates process-group termination after a grace period; this is not a Python API for proving arbitrary descendant absence. process/closed requires an exit code and closed output streams; entry removal occurs later after retention. A terminate response's tracked running state, closed event or group kill is therefore not complete allocation release or container deletion.

Receipts and next action: retain exact executable/image bytes and their hashes, target/version/configuration hashes, requested and observed resource policy, supervisor-issued identities, command/result evidence, cleanup observations and explicit PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED/NOT RUN for each gate. Redact secrets; simulated identities and caller-authored booleans do not become observed facts. The next runtime action is currently BLOCKED at CP-1: establish an approved supervisor on this cloud with effective quotas and independent cleanup. No live-budget request alone resolves it. CP-2 can proceed independently of SDK/auth setup once its own prerequisites are actually met. Continue permitted source work without pretending additional pure tests close D00. Keep A1/A2/A3, R19-F01 and incomplete TW scenarios visible.

Source-only lifecycle fixture contract · revision 33

Goal and selected approach: turn the documented stop/release failure cases into an executable, pure reference evaluator for D00, AC-02/04/06 and the future runtime adapter. A table alone cannot prevent fixture regressions; a live admission controller or another durable ledger would depend on unresolved runtime gates. The bounded choice is automation.lifecycle.evaluate_lifecycle_fixture(run, allocations, observations) plus standard-library tests. It performs no I/O, scheduling, persistence, SDK adaptation, discovery or actual release. No CLI or existing journal uses it.

Inputs: plain dictionaries/lists with exact fields and schema version 1. Run fields are schema_version, run_id. Each allocation has schema_version, run_id, allocation_id, generation, container_id, process_ids. Declare 1–1,000 allocations, each with 1–1,000 opaque process-instance IDs, at most 10,000 processes overall. Within each typed namespace, allocation IDs, container IDs and process-instance IDs are unique across this fixed inventory; cross-namespace spelling overlap is permitted because observation kinds select the resource namespace; an OS PID alone cannot establish process identity. Generation is an exact integer from 1 through 2,147,483,647. IDs are nonblank control-free Unicode of at most 128 code points. No declaration claims completeness or trusted authority.

Observations: at most 10,000 ordered records, each with schema_version, event_id, run_id, allocation_id, generation, kind, resource_id, outcome, evidence_ref, evidence_sha256. Kinds are fence, process_exit, container_delete, reload, stop_requested, stop_acknowledged, owner_finished, archive, unsubscribe. Outcomes are exactly succeeded, failed, unknown. Process/container kinds name their exact declared resource; all other kinds name the allocation itself. Every run, allocation, generation and resource must match. Evidence references are opaque control-free text up to 1,024 code points with a lowercase SHA256 digest; nothing fetches or authenticates them. All input is validated before projection; malformed or undeclared records fail with ValueError. Order is fixture order, not verified provider chronology.

Fence and uncertainty: a new successful fence starts a fresh proof window for that allocation; it discards earlier process/container proof. A reload observation of any outcome discards the fence and cleanup proof. Successful process exits and container deletion qualify only after the current fence; all declared processes and the exact container are required independently. Stop requests, acknowledgements, finished/idle-owner labels, archive and unsubscribe never count as cleanup. Version 1 deliberately keeps any failed or unknown observation sticky for the affected allocation throughout the supplied history; later success cannot erase it. A future reconciliation extension needs a reviewed contract. Exact duplicate event IDs are inert globally, including after reload or uncertainty; changed reuse is rejected.

Output: deterministic fresh per-allocation structures in declaration order report the current fence, qualifying process IDs in declaration order, container proof, sorted reasons and synthetic_release_prerequisites_satisfied. Every declared allocation remains in occupied_allocations, even when the synthetic boolean is true. All reports permanently set execution_enabled=false, admission_permitted=false, release_permitted=false, inventory_complete=false. There is no released/available/admissible capacity or runtime-ready output. Caller-declared evidence does not prove identity, coverage, actual process exit, protected start/reload exclusion, lineage, reservation atomicity or safe resource reuse.

Coordination: baseline clean main 81d48874fef251c6620080d16265f8fc53c834d5, verified against private GitHub. Original author /root/advance_runner_preflight held source ownership v7. After native roster reset, successor /root/complete_runner_fixture_review holds source ownership v8 and is the sole writer of the frozen module/tests, existing guide/plan/handoff and Git index. Supported interruption lookups returned not_found for the prior author/reviewer, and no overlapping owner or Git lock was found; process inspection is limited to the available namespace. Supervisor /root delegated bounded design decisions after independent challenge. Original contract reviewer /root/advance_runner_preflight/review_lifecycle_fixture_contract is historical. Final independent reviewer /root/review_runner_lifecycle has read-only ownership v2; no prior review completion is inferred from roster absence. Dot Chat: platform-managed settings; no model specified. No Issue Lead counter or live runtime ledger is invented. No branch, worktree or former owner chat is used.

Implementation and checks: independent LF-C1–C5 require permanent occupied capacity, exact resource/generation binding, fence/reload windows, immutable replay/sticky uncertainty and strict bounds. The above contract accepts all five. Manual trace: bounded input capture/validation → immutable event identity and exact inventory association → ordered fence/reload/cleanup projection → fresh permanently denied report. Write failing fixtures first, implement only this pure boundary, then focused/full unittest verification and independent frozen-source review/adversarial QA. Test incomplete/complete proof, partial teardown, stale identity, reload at each point, unknown cleanup, replay, mixed allocations, bounds, input/output isolation and permanent denial. Commit/push the verified logical change and generated changelog separately.

Limits: source tests do not close A1/A2/A3, R19-F01, callback wire validation, supported authenticated isolation or any full TW runtime scenario. Runtime execution remains disabled. Both live SDK windows remain expired; original historical usage UNKNOWN, six charged additional turns and four expired unused turns remain unchanged. No SDK/client/app-server/model call, process/container probe, installation, credential access or security-setting change is authorized by this contract.

RNR-D03 early store preparation: holds and original deadline

Goal, architecture and scope: persist run-wide and repository-qualified issue holds plus the original run start/deadline through reopen, using one bounded standard-library SQLite journal. This is early source-only AC-04 work under D03, not enforcement, authentication or full recovery. The existing operation and usage journals cannot represent this contract without conflating an operation result with a hold. A dedicated caller-owned database is the smallest bounded preparation; integration into one transactional controller store remains separate work. Do not add a generic storage framework or CLI/runtime path.

Immutable run: initialize(connection, run) requires an empty unmarked database and exact fields schema_version=1, run_id, started_at, deadline_at. IDs are nonempty control-free Unicode up to 128 characters. Times are bounded UTC strings up to 64 characters, with deadline strictly later than start; original text is retained. There is no extension, reset, clock, remaining-allowance or mutation API. Reinitialization is rejected even for an identical run. These declared boundaries are not proof of an approval, original allowance or current authority.

Append-only events: record(connection, event) requires exactly schema_version=1, event_id, hold_id, run_id, scope, repository, issue, state, evidence_ref, evidence_sha256. Scope is run or issue. Run scope requires null repository/issue; issue scope requires a valid repository up to 4,096 characters and issue 1–2,147,483,647. State is held or released. Evidence references are control-free text up to 1,024 characters; SHA256 is lowercase. Both input and stored canonical JSON are limited to 32,768 bytes. Capture flat scalar dictionaries before SQLite callbacks. Run ID must match metadata.

Identity and release: a held event creates one previously unseen hold ID. A release must match an existing currently held ID and its exact run/scope/repository/issue. Distinct holds for the same scope are independent; releasing one never clears another or a run-wide hold. Reusing a hold ID for a new hold, or issuing a second release under a new event ID, fails. Global event IDs are immutable. Exact event replay is inert after complete stored-history validation, before state/capacity checks, even after release. A stale replay cannot clear a later hold. No event proves the supplied approval/evidence or authenticates its caller.

Bounds and output: retain at most 10,000 events and 1,000 distinct repository/issue scope pairs across all history, including released holds. At the event cap, even a new otherwise valid release is rejected, leaving that hold active; never delete history or bypass the bound to clear it. snapshot(connection) returns the immutable run, complete ordered events, holds in creation order with their derived state/hold and release event IDs, and active hold IDs. It always reports source_hold_journal, execution_enabled=false and dispatch_permitted=false. No unheld or released result permits dispatch. Full schema, contiguous sequences, SQL/payload identity, run association and transitions are revalidated on every call; corrupt state fails without repair.

Plan and trace: existing bootstrap/CLI remains unchanged and does not import the journal. Trace caller input capture → exact validation → shared trusted-connection checks → owned transaction → complete committed history → identity/transition/capacity decision → append/commit or cleanup. Reuse automation/sqlite_support.py unchanged. Implement only automation/hold_journal.py and tests/test_automation_hold_journal.py, plus this plan, guide and handoff. Write failing tests first, then run PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_automation_hold_journal.py -v; expect absent-module assertions, then a green focused suite after implementation. Broaden to the full existing unittest suite, independent frozen-source review/QA, document/staged checks and scoped commit/push, followed by the required generated changelog commit.

Review focus: reopen cannot extend deadlines or erase holds; repository collisions and overlapping holds remain separate; old release replay cannot affect later holds; all-history caps include released scopes and allow only exact replay at capacity; invalid schema/history and interruptions preserve committed facts. Cover maximum Unicode records, callback mutation, shared-cache isolation, caller transactions and uncertain COMMIT read-back. The shared transaction helper's existing tests remain applicable. Independent contract review by /root/resume_runner_cloud/review_runner_hold_contract accepted this bounded design and HJ-C1–C4 clarifications before source implementation. There is no SDK/model use, runtime probe, credential transfer, installation or budget renewal.

Source verification: all 13 new focused tests failed before implementation and pass afterward. The full existing suite ran 247 tests with one optional pinned git-cliff skip. Independent final reviewer /root/review_runner_hold_store accepted the frozen source/tests, independently passed all 13 tests and eight additional adversarial checks, including committed snapshot/isolation and failed-transaction boundaries. No blocking finding remains. These source results do not close D00, enable execution or establish physical crash durability.

RNR-D03 early store preparation: operation intents and receipts

Goal and scope: preserve a fixed logical operation and its observation history across restart, without dispatching, authorizing, retrying or claiming its external effect. This implements a bounded part of the existing transactions/effects contract and AC-04 before D02 integration. Only synthetic fixtures use the journal. No runtime, SDK, CLI, network transport, credential, production path or new database is created by the library.

Approaches: another pure report would not preserve uncertain outcomes after reopening; a complete controller ledger would couple unproven ownership/admission/lifecycle interfaces. The selected source-only journal uses a caller-supplied trusted dedicated SQLite connection, an immutable run ID, immutable operation intents and append-only receipt records. Reuse the existing journal's proven connection/transaction boundary through a small shared SQLite helper rather than maintain two diverging copies. The observation journal's public behavior and schema remain unchanged.

Interfaces: proposed automation/operation_journal.py exports initialize(connection, run_id), register(connection, intent), record(connection, receipt) and snapshot(connection). The three mutation functions return None only after commit. Initialization requires an empty unmarked database; reopen uses the other operations. A new application marker, version 1 and exact dedicated schema are verified on every call. No migration, repair or automatic retry is performed. All connection settings and owned-transaction cleanup rules of the observation journal, including disabled read_uncommitted, apply unchanged.

Immutable intent: exactly schema_version=1, request_id, run_id, repository, issue, delivery_attempt_id, action, caller_id, ownership_version and request_sha256. Text IDs and action are nonempty control-free Unicode strings of at most 128 characters; repository uses the existing owner/name validator with a 4,096-character limit; issue is a positive integer up to 2^31-1; ownership_version is a positive integer up to 2^63-1; hashes are lowercase SHA256. The request hash binds caller-defined request bytes; those bytes, commands, secrets and credentials are not stored or executed. The caller/run/ownership fields are declarations, not authenticated authority. The intent run_id must match the journal. Public register/record calls capture a private flat dictionary before validation and SQLite calls; all accepted fields are immutable scalars, and that same capture supplies serialization, lookup, transition and insertion values. Later caller-dictionary mutation cannot split persisted identity from payload. This is not a general callback-security boundary. Exact intent replay is inert. Reusing a request ID with changed intent fails atomically. One logical key (repository, issue, delivery_attempt_id, action) maps to one request ID within the fixed run, preventing an accidental new ID from silently replacing that logical operation.

Receipt: exactly schema_version=1, receipt_id, request_id, kind, state, evidence_ref and evidence_sha256. IDs use the same bounds; evidence_ref is control-free text of at most 1,024 characters and is never fetched. kind is start, result or reconciliation. A new intent begins pending. A receipt must reference an existing request. A start receipt requires state=in_flight and changes only pending to in_flight; a result changes in_flight to confirmed, failed or unknown. A reconciliation receipt changes in_flight or unknown to confirmed, failed or unknown. Unknown cannot return to pending or in_flight. Confirmed and failed are terminal in this slice; retry-attempt scheduling is deferred and no API may reset their histories. Version 1 records one execution attempt per logical operation, not a reusable retry ledger. Future retries require a separately reviewed attempt/reconciliation extension or migration that preserves the same logical request and prior history. Creating another delivery-attempt identity is not a safe workaround for retrying an uncertain effect. Global receipt IDs are immutable: after full stored-history validation, exact complete-record replay is checked before transition/capacity checks and is inert even after later transitions or at capacity; a changed record under the same receipt ID fails. Preserve original receipt order and all distinct accepted receipts.

Interpretation and bounds: states are validated caller observations, not proof of remote outcomes. Snapshot returns schema_version=1, mode=source_operation_journal, run_id, execution_enabled=false, dispatch_permitted=false, retry_permitted=false, and operations in first-registration order with their complete intent/receipt history, derived state and reconciliation_required for in_flight/unknown. No state grants execution or acceptance. Bound storage to 1,000 intents and 10,000 receipts, with a 32,768-byte canonical JSON bound per intent/receipt enforced both before insertion and on reload, exact sequence validation, schema identity checks and full history revalidation before any operation. Validate a prospective transition and limits before insertion. Invalid input/history raises ValueError; SQLite/storage errors propagate; failed COMMIT remains uncertain until read-back. The trusted database is not tamper resistant or an authorization source. Physical crash durability, backups, host locks, actual effect reconciliation and runtime integration remain unproven.

Ownership and acceptance: native owner /root/advance_runner_next_slice retains sole source/docs/index ownership, v5, under coordinator /root's explicit shared-contract decision authority. Baseline is clean synchronized 82c7841c04f40682aaf430c386bd4404338f239b. No overlapping writer exists. A fresh nonauthor challenges this contract before implementation and independently reviews/validates the frozen result. Owned files are the new operation journal and focused tests, a shared SQLite helper, the observation journal's mechanical helper import and interruption-test adaptation, and existing guide/plan/handoff. No unrelated changes, Issue Lead counter or runtime ledger is created. Dot Chat: platform-managed settings; no model specified.

  1. Independently challenge identity, allowed transitions, idempotent replay, limits, transaction/schema integrity and the shared-helper extraction before source changes.
  2. Trace caller validation through the dedicated connection/transaction, exact schema/history revalidation, proposed change and commit/rollback. Write failing tests for durable reopen, fixed logical/request/receipt IDs, all transitions, uncertain outcomes, exact replay after terminal state, caller-state preservation, malformed persisted history, capacity and interrupted writes.
  3. Implement only the accepted source contract using Python standard library. Preserve observation-journal behavior and valid existing evidence; run focused operation/observation tests and the complete repository suite.
  4. Freeze for independent review/QA and close findings; record exact evidence in the current guide/plan/handoff. Stage-check, commit/push each verified increment with expected-head compare-and-swap and exact tree/commit verification; regenerate changelog separately.

Source verification: implemented with 16 regression-first tests failing on the absent module, then passing. Independent review found OJ-F1: a SQLite callback could mutate a caller dictionary after serialization but before identity/transition checks, committing unreadable history. Three new failing regressions were corrected by privately capturing the validated flat input. All 41 focused journal tests now pass, including the existing 22 usage tests and a 75-case operation transition matrix; the full 229-test repository suite passes with one optional git-cliff integration skipped. Independent recheck closed OJ-F1, reran all 41 tests, and passed three additional early-callback dictionary-replacement probes. No blocking source finding remains. Publication is verified separately by exact local/GitHub commit and tree read-back; runtime acceptance is unchanged.

Status: independent contract challenge accepted the bounded approach. OJ-C1 adds symmetric input/reload bounds and maximum-size round-trip tests; OJ-C2 makes associations, start/state combinations and replay precedence explicit; OJ-C3 distinguishes one-attempt storage from future retry scheduling. These clarifications are accepted before implementation. Reviewer is /root/advance_runner_next_slice/review_operation_journal, canonical development-standards-rnr-d03-operation-reviewer-qa, with no write ownership. D00/D02, A1/A2/A3, callback wire validation, descendant lifecycle and R19-F01 stay open. Both live SDK allowances remain expired. No journal record can renew those allowances or establish dispatch authority.

RNR-D03 maintenance: committed journal snapshots

Contract and diagnosis: the journal's single-snapshot promise requires SQLite's connection-wide read_uncommitted setting to be zero. A real shared-cache fixture on baseline 8bd839bbc64ed3b4e871060742a716e063cc2036 returned an uncommitted observation of 999, then 2 after the other connection rolled back. Explicit BEGIN alone did not prevent that dirty read. All three public journal operations must reject a nonzero setting before BEGIN or schema/data mutation, without silently changing caller settings. The existing active-caller-transaction rejection remains first. Reject the unsupported setting even without shared cache; do not claim that enabling it always permits dirty reads.

Dot Chat: platform-managed settings; no model specified. No new issue, runner ledger or Issue Lead counter is created.

Confirm the real shared-cache fixture exposes an uncommitted record through raw SQL before asserting journal rejection. Retain default-isolation success after the caller restores its setting.
  3. Add only the central isolation guard, run focused and full tests, then freeze source/tests for nonauthor review and QA. Record the result here and in the existing handoff.

Independent nonauthor review passed the exact frozen source/tests with no findings; independent QA reran all 22 tests plus 27 alias/nonzero-setting/operation combinations, checking rejection before BEGIN, unchanged serialized database and preservation of caller transactions. RNR-D03 early source preparation: SQLite observation journal

Goal and spec: preserve the already reviewed normalized cumulative observations across database reopen and replay. This bounded source task advances the accounting/store rows of the existing contracts and AC-04 without implementing runtime dispatch, reservations, deadlines, backup reconciliation or complete accounting. The caller supplies an already opened, trusted, dedicated SQLite connection. No production file path, credential, enrollment, SDK, CLI command or network transport is created.

Approach: use Python standard-library sqlite3 and the existing pure summarize_cumulative_usage function. More pure reports would not preserve observations across restart; implementing a complete runner store would prematurely couple unresolved authority and lifecycle contracts. The selected small journal preserves only immutable expected streams and normalized observation payloads. It is not an authoritative run ledger or a trustworthy attribution source.

Interfaces: automation/usage_journal.py exports initialize(connection, expected_streams) -> None, append(connection, events) -> None, and summarize(connection) -> dict. A fresh empty dedicated database with zero identity/version markers is initialized explicitly; existing journals, including identical expectations, are rejected by initialize. A journal-specific application marker, schema version and exact table/index definitions are checked on every call; reopening uses summarize/append, not initialization. The expected stream list is immutable. Canonical JSON payloads are stored in first-receipt order; exact replay adds no row, while conflicting identity variants remain preserved for the existing projection. Every result stays incomplete and non-authorizing.

Connection and transaction boundary: the caller owns connection lifecycle and physical-path permissions. Require standard row/text factories, no active transaction, no attached database or temporary objects, and no unsafe rollback/synchronization mode. Do not silently reconfigure those properties. Qualify all data access with the main schema. Initialize and append use explicit SQL BEGIN IMMEDIATE, COMMIT and rollback on exceptions or interruption, preserving any rollback failure; never use executescript. Summarize reads one explicit transaction snapshot. No callback, runtime or network operation occurs within a transaction. Reject unrecognized schema versions, non-journal schema objects and invalid persisted records without repair or mutation. Commit failure has an uncertain outcome until read-back; the API does not retry automatically.

Bounds and integrity: retain the pure projection's record validation, at most 1,000 declared streams and 10,000 retained unique payload variants, with bounded canonical JSON and aggregate counter overflow checks. Validate each bounded raw input batch before deduplication, then the complete combined projection before inserting any new rows. Canonical deduplication uses the entire normalized record, including identity, without trimming/case-folding/Unicode normalization; retain first insertion and expected-stream order. Revalidate canonical persisted records before use. Append returns None only after commit; summarize returns the unchanged projection. Invalid contracts raise ValueError; SQLite/storage failures propagate, never becoming an empty report. Preserve exact duplicates at the cap and reject a capacity-exceeding or invalid batch atomically. This database is trusted input, not protection against an agent/operator replacing or forging it; no tamper resistance, completeness, host lock, physical crash durability, network-filesystem support or cross-host failover is established.

Ownership and review: native cloud owner v3 alone writes source/tests/guide/this plan/handoff and the Git index. A nonauthor challenges the contract before dependent code and independently reviews and runs the frozen implementation. Existing bootstrap/CLI/accounting interfaces remain unchanged. Do not create another plan, issue or execution mode. No real runtime database is initialized.

  1. Trace caller connection checks, explicit transaction, schema/record validation, canonical replay deduplication, pure projection and commit/rollback. Record independent contract findings and resolve them before implementation.
  2. RNR-D03 early source preparation: cumulative usage projection

Status: the pure source projection is implemented and independently reviewed after contract challenge. This is early D03 source preparation only; no durable recovery or runtime acceptance is claimed. This is only the early source/decision testing allowed by RNR-D03 and the accounting row of the reviewed design. It does not implement D02, persistence, admission, retries or release.

Choice: first implement one pure standard-library function, summarize_cumulative_usage(events, expected_streams), in automation/accounting.py, with synthetic tests. Defer SQLite until observation/conflict semantics are settled; adding a database now would not establish runtime recovery. Defer deltas and mixed accounting modes until a separate reviewed coverage rule exists. No CLI command, SDK import, file/network access, real state creation or execution path is added.

Expected streams: each record has exactly schema_version=1, source and session_id. Pairs must be unique; version 1 accepts one source namespace per report and at most 1,000 sessions. The source is a caller-declared provider/account namespace, not a collector name, authenticated identity or proof of correct account attribution/exhaustive coverage. An empty expectation list is unknown history, not zero usage.

Normalized event: exactly schema_version=1, source, session_id, turn_id, event_id, mode="cumulative", scope="session", metric="total_tokens", unit="tokens", value, evidence_ref and evidence_sha256. Value is a nonnegative integer up to 2^63-1, or explicit null; booleans/floats are rejected. IDs are nonempty control-free strings of at most 128 characters; evidence references are bounded to 1,024 characters and never fetched, and hashes are lowercase SHA256. At most 10,000 events are accepted. Unsupported fields, versions, modes, scopes/metrics, unknown streams or aggregate integer overflow fail closed. These are synthetic normalized records, not a claimed mapping of SDK events.

Projection rules: preserve caller receipt order. Deduplicate on source/session/turn/event identity; identical payload replay does not increase the observed aggregate. A changed payload under one identity is a reconciliation conflict. Keep the highest observed numeric counter for that session, including the higher conflicting observation, without calling it verified spending. Distinct events with equal counters do not increase the observed aggregate. Unknown/conflict reasons remain sticky within the report; a later numeric observation cannot erase them. Unique event count counts identity keys, not payload variants. Numeric maxima and their sum are permutation-invariant even where receipt-order warnings differ. A lower new counter retains the prior maximum and flags regression-or-reordering because provider ordering is unproven. A new turn does not reset a session counter. Explicit unknown observations and absent expected streams remain visible. Usage has no ownership-filter input, so old-owner spending is not silently discarded. Different sessions within the one declared source namespace contribute separate observed maxima.

Output: schema version 1, mode="source_projection", execution_enabled=false, per-stream observed_tokens (maximum or null), unique_event_count and reconciliation reasons, plus the sum of known maxima as observed_tokens (null when none are known). Never label that sum a verified lower bound or complete charge when observations conflict. total_tokens always remains null, complete always remains false, and coverage_unverified always remains a reason: this slice cannot establish missing-event coverage or trusted completeness. Return no money amount or remaining-budget/admission decision. Inputs remain unchanged.

Acceptance tests: empty/missing/explicit-zero/unknown history; identical and conflicting duplicate identities; repeated cumulative values; resumed turns; multiple sessions; lower/out-of-order observations; unsupported delta/mixed scope; unknown/duplicate streams; negative/bool/float/overflow values; version/field/string/count bounds; input immutability; and invariant non-authorizing output. Review the actual interfaces before writing implementation, then use failing tests, focused/full verification, nonauthor code review and prompt scoped commit/push.

Implementation task: create only automation/accounting.py and tests/test_automation_accounting.py, reusing existing validators without changing their contracts. Keep the existing plan/handoff and operator guide current. Python standard library only; native execution method and one source writer remain unchanged.

D01 live transport design accepted for source implementation

The installed gh API client follows redirects, may refresh credentials and transforms JSON bodies, so it cannot supply the strict exact-repository raw-response boundary. The proposed replacement uses its supported auth token --hostname github.com --no-refresh accessor once, with a bounded coordinator passing credentials only through memory/anonymous pipes to installed curl. This is a proposed credential-access exception, not current authorization. No credential was accessed; the user's planned sign-in does not waive the existing restriction. OS keyring access may prompt/unlock despite gh's prompt suppression; the runner must not automate or bypass it.

Keep the existing snapshot adapter and offline CLI. A reviewed source-only transport may use fake credential/HTTP runners while its production factory remains disabled. Proposed live bounds: ten exact enrolled-repository issue/dependency GET attempts, 90 seconds total including five seconds reserved for cleanup, 20 seconds per request clipped to remaining time, 1 MiB response bodies and bounded header/aggregate output. No redirects, retries, refresh, ancillary requests or writes. Preserve actual status/body/Link and fixed redacted errors. Authorize dependency numbers only after full listing validation through a narrow optional adapter callback; never fetch foreign dependencies. Context validation precedes helper access. A live advisory result never enables issue execution.

Review corrections R1–R4: one supervised worker owns the entire live fetch/normalize/select/serialize pipeline and maps all failures to fixed redacted codes, including malformed server JSON. The 90-second interval begins after existing local context validation; work ends by +85 and cleanup by +90. Local context validation and final ordinary CLI output delivery are excluded, so this is not a whole-command time guarantee. Success requires a complete capped result, clean worker exit and confirmed owned-helper cleanup. Result encoding/IPC is capped at 4 MiB; no partial preview or automatic retry follows failure or delivery loss.

Core-dump suppression is mandatory before credential access; every credential-bearing worker/helper gets the explicit secret/debug environment policy, including removal of TLS key logging. Helper stdin writes and all output drains are nonblocking and deadline-aware, with fixed numeric per-stream/aggregate caps in the frozen contract. Preserve supported proxy/trust settings. A process-local permit binds exact repository/run/limits/expiry and is consumed before access, never restored; terminal failure forbids further I/O and close is idempotent. Production minting stays absent/disabled, and synthetic permits cannot activate production. This protects against accidental reuse, not a malicious same-user process. Framing rejects conflicting/duplicate length, encoding or Link fields and all trailers; curl yields transfer-decoded representation bytes. Real-client framing/proxy behavior remains separately unqualified.

Supporting corrected frozen proposal: runner-d01-live-20261115/transport-contract-s5.md, SHA256 981cfc52ff3a18b7010a6e8a520a503d5e622dc21c03deed7ab7e93f87259304; independent targeted review closed R1–R4 at design level; disabled source-only implementation may proceed. The optional dependency callback accepts exactly one unique positive-integer tuple, including empty, after full listing validation. Main will prepare and review the source before requesting the precise one-run credential exception; no authentication readiness or live coverage is claimed.

Source findings: 66 additional upstream bodies were verified against the retained Git tree at 086ccc833bc9cf01024ad2dd6af7dbd421b6bf9a. Empty environments do not disable host hooks or legacy notify, plugin disabling does not close all extensions, and private stdio does not itself disable persisted remote control.   1. Bootstrap and ownership: protected TaskPacket/RunPolicy inputs determine an immutable BootstrapSpec, including role, owner version, original limits, generation, runtime/config/protocol fingerprints and resource identity. Reserve runtime capacity in Store before external creation. An injected BootstrapOps owns private byte handles and observed process-instance binding. Its state flow is reserved → runtime-dispatching → initialized → policy-verified → fresh-thread-created → registered → acknowledged. Missing/lost/contradictory receipts fence and retain UNKNOWN without repeat creation. Only a committed SessionBinding may register Assignment; packet/resource/owner acknowledgement must precede its first ordinary work turn or command. The sole pre-ACK exception is one explicitly typed ownership_ack turn after committed policy-verified binding and registration; it consumes normal turn allowance/capacity with unchanged dispatch/server-binding/UNKNOWN rules. It cannot execute commands, expand resources or transfer ownership. Validate the exact task/session/role/resources/policy/owner version before committing ACK. Failure/loss cannot replay it. Host ACK is limited to host-owned orchestration and cannot substitute for a delegated owner. Workload messages cannot register authority.
  2. Wire scope: retain fixed protected turn/start and server binding. Add only pinned, strictly discriminated initialization/config/thread/turn observations required for this path, with explicit envelope/item/count/byte limits. User/agent messages, reasoning, plan and the exact rnr_command item are observations, never alternate dispatch. Unexpected effectful tools, configuration warnings, internal wakes or unknown fields fence.   3. Resource lifetimes: distinguish assignment runtime allocation, persistent owner workspace and unique disposable workload allocation per command/check. Store counts pending and UNKNOWN runtime/workload reservations separately, including real reviewer/helper roles; reserve required review capacity before owners consume it. Infrastructure is inventoried separately. A healthy command's confirmed workload cleanup does not fence its runtime; generation/runtime uncertainty still does. Introduce an explicit incompatible synthetic schema version and refuse older stores unchanged, without inventing production migration.
  4. Supervisor: fixed typed create/inspect/start/stop/remove and bounded output operations receive immutable spec, exact instance/name/labels, operation identity and original deadline. A short local lock serializes one-shot ticket consumption and fence state; it is never held across database access, pipe writes, daemon calls or joins. The lock-protected one-shot dispatch_committed transition is distinct from daemon_submission_observed and is already potentially externally effective. Fence closes all future commitments and cancels still-cancellable committed work. A commitment whose no-effect status cannot be proved remains occupied UNKNOWN even when submission was not observed before fence return; late effects remain possible across the check-to-call gap. No new start commitment follows a fence, including after a late create. Never replay or release from one absence observation; reconcile and clean exact late identities. A local fence receipt proves closure of future commitments only. The stronger guarantee of no actual external submission/effect after fence return remains an explicit operational blocker until an adapter primitive proves it.
  5. Release and workspace: typed release evidence binds the exact ticket/instance, resolved create/start submissions, stopped process namespace, removal and independent absence observation. Store commits its evidence digest and decrements capacity once; stale/duplicate/conflicting evidence cannot create extra credit. A contradiction after release atomically places a run-wide admission hold, preserves the original credit/history and forbids further admission pending reconciliation. Seal the workspace in Store before quiescence checks to block new writer workloads; reconcile every existing writer authority before freezing content, modes, symlinks and untracked files into an immutable candidate manifest. Owner and reviewer/check mounts differ; checks use separate credential-free workloads. Offline child processes are allowed within the approved container quotas, with protected provider/controller/native-agent routes inaccessible.
  6. Implementation ownership: a fresh Issue Lead owns shared Controller/Store records and integration tests, and must publish exact record fields/method results before dependent writes. That Lead creates BS-1 (control_bootstrap.py, bounded protocol extensions and tests) and BS-2 (control_supervisor.py, control_workspace.py and tests). Children cannot edit shared Controller/Store files. Main owns canonical docs and Git. These are distinct registrations, avoiding a launch-before-binding cycle. Healthy workload release must not inherit the old Controller generation fence. No legacy entry point may bypass binding/ACK. Teamwork compatibility checks

Complete TW-01–TW-10 scenarios: NOT RUN. Revision 19 adds partial live SDK evidence and a blocking admission observation above. These checks do not run an implemented runner or provide full runtime sign-off. The official SDK documentation describes session controls; that alone does not establish this integration. Record actual commands, runtime/version, identities, snapshots, results, and limitations as each authorized scenario is executed; partial preflight evidence does not complete a scenario.

V5 evidence applicability, 14 November 2026: the seven scripted direct-stdio scenarios add bounded observations relevant to TW-07 (inspected container policy and retained-tool denial), TW-08 (transport death preserves UNKNOWN/no replay) and TW-10 (one-shot reservation and observed counts/deadlines). They do not complete those scenarios: authenticated authority, alternate-start prevention, full process-tree stop/recovery, restart accounting and all-role limits remain unproved. No native child creation or multi-owner scheduling occurred, so TW-01–TW-05 and TW-09 receive no new runner-level acceptance. Independent receipt review supports evidence quality, not an implemented TW-06 acceptance workflow. No full TW scenario or supported runner mode is enabled by this experiment.

ScenarioRequired observable result
TW-01 — Choose the right modeA small task stays with the Lead / Orchestrator. An ordinary delegated issue gets a fresh dedicated owner. A complex issue gets an Issue Lead with at least two substantive task owners. A new issue cannot reuse another issue's implementer.
TW-02 — Real child creationThe actual Issue Lead calls the spawning tool; returned IDs, creation events, and parent evidence identify it as creator. A Lead / Orchestrator attempt to create those children is rejected. Missing nested support prevents complex-mode dispatch.
TW-03 — Four allocations and review capacityPrimary agents, nested agents, and support agents share one enforced total. A full implementation wave cannot start another child or strand review/QA. Checkpoint/release/resume preserves ownership and is demonstrated with real runtime capacity reports.
TW-04 — Feedback during active workOrdinary feedback waits for a safe checkpoint and follows the supervisor route. Duplicate or stale messages cannot replay changes. An authorized urgent stop halts the affected process tree and saves work before any reassignment.
TW-05 — Shared files and resourcesTwo children requesting the same file, generator output, test database, port, or Git write cannot run conflicting operations. A changed interface pauses affected consumers until the Lead decision, new revision, and owner acknowledgement are recorded.
TW-06 — Independent acceptanceLead-authored interfaces receive independent review before dependent work. Required Reviewer and QA identities are nonauthors; complex/high-risk work uses separate agents. Child results alone cannot accept the combined issue, and new integration code gets independent coverage.
TW-07 — Settings and accessEffective settings match the applicable policy, both parent ceilings, or explicit scoped exceptions. Dot-led mode supplies no model identifiers. Container checks block host secrets, controller state, Git publishing credentials, Docker access, and unauthorized source/Git writes even with the configured Full Access runtime preset. Controller Git operations cannot run candidate code through hooks, filters or untrusted configuration; required project checks run in isolation.
TW-08 — Recovery and correctionCrashes preserve attempts, messages, original creator evidence, counters, and uncertain effects. Old writes stop before a versioned transfer. A persistent defect reaches a fresh Correction Implementer through the correct supervisor, within remaining limits, then independent recheck.
TW-09 — Lead absence and continuationAn idle chat is not treated as an available supervisor. Supported wake/continuation and ownership transfer are demonstrated; otherwise affected dispatch pauses with a complete handoff. New-chat continuation preserves the run and finished contributions without relying on predecessor agents.
TW-10 — Counters and completion gatesAgent-turn, usage, retry, and time records include every role and survive restart. Unobservable metrics are reported as unsupported. PR creation, verified integration, acceptance, and deployment remain distinct; no label or success message bypasses required evidence.

Required tests

Use unit tests for decisions, real SQLite and temporary Git repositories for state behavior, and controlled fake provider responses for failure cases. Cover the compatibility scenarios above with actual runtime evidence where required; mocked creation or stop events cannot prove those runtime capabilities.

Cover:

  • Approved versus unapproved issues.
  • Native dependencies, pagination, cycles, and inaccessible blockers.
  • Duplicate starts and stale ownership.
  • Crash before and after each significant state transition.
  • Rollback from a backup older than a completed dispatch or remote write: preserve newer receipts, ownership and cumulative limits, reconcile post-backup effects, and block dependent writes if that history cannot be recovered.
  • A remote write that succeeds but loses its response.
  • Failed tests, unresolved review findings, and repeated lack of progress.
  • Changed requirements versus ordinary status changes.
  • Cancellation of agents, tests, and their child processes.
  • Retry and usage totals surviving restart.
  • Required commit hooks running only in isolation; host Git rejecting hooks, executable filters and agent-controlled configuration; source rewrites returning to a new candidate; validation rejecting source drift in a writable test copy while allowing declared generated outputs; post-merge correction using a new delivery attempt while retries reuse its existing PR.
  • User pause, stop, cancellation and supersession resisting timer/queued-message resumption; saved wall-clock deadlines surviving human waits and restarts; issue reservations released only after confirmed stopping and saved closure evidence.
  • Resume packets with missing sources, stale owners, changed instructions, closed issues, exhausted limits, user holds and lost dispatch acknowledgements; no duplicate first turn.
  • Feedback before merge, after merge but before acceptance, after accepted closure, and after premature closure; duplicate or disputed findings; later regressions affecting dependency readiness.
  • Authorized follow-up issue creation, duplicate detection, missing publication permission, lost responses and backlink failures; no automatic new-scope approval or reset of run/finding limits.
  • Current versus archived context selection, exact historical-evidence lookup, missing status and stale index links; archive/index updates interrupted after GitHub closure; reactivation only for corrected premature closure or an explicit authorized document change.
  • Attempts to access controller credentials or the Docker socket.
  • Untrusted issue text trying to change permissions or check commands.
  • Permission denial with an already-authorized tool available, a pending approval request, a declined request, or a platform restriction; verify that only the affected action waits and access is checked before resuming.
  • Usage reported without a charge, missing usage fields, and account-wide data; verify that reports do not invent a per-run cost or claim unsupported money limits.
  • Branch changes made by a human.
  • PR closure without merge and merge while the runner is offline.
  • Missing manual acceptance evidence; early automatic issue closure; closure denied or successful with a lost response; cancelled closure that must not satisfy dependencies. Acceptance without confirmed closure cannot unblock a dependent issue; closed issues with pending archive/release effects cannot make a resumed run report full completion. Independent work may use confirmed-free resources without waiting for unrelated maintenance.
  • Existing standards commands continuing to work without runner dependencies.

Live demonstration

Use a disposable test repository and real Codex execution:

  1. Prepare a small feature PRD and issue set.
  2. Approve two independent issues and a third that depends on one of them.
  3. Complete work through checks, independent review/QA, and PR creation using the actual Lead hierarchy.
  4. Exercise one complex issue with two substantive children created by its Issue Lead, then separate review and QA in capacity-limited waves.
  5. Interrupt an agent and the controller, then resume.
  6. Inject a temporary network failure and a failed check.
  7. Confirm no duplicate PR or repeated external action appears.
  8. Have a person merge the prerequisite PR. Confirm that merge alone does not make the dependent issue eligible while required prerequisite acceptance is incomplete.
  9. Read back the merged revision, complete the prerequisite's required integrated and manual acceptance, confirm its authorized GitHub closure, and finalize its resources. Then recheck the dependent issue's authority, current contracts, other dependencies and remaining limits before confirming eligibility. Missing or failed prerequisite acceptance must keep that dependent work blocked.
  10. Complete feature acceptance and prepare release-readiness evidence.
  11. Confirm the runner neither merges nor deploys automatically.

Verify operation separately on WSL2 and native Linux. A WSL test does not establish native Linux results.

Rollout

  • First use: preview only.
  • Then: one issue in the disposable repository.
  • Then: recovery and remote-write tests.
  • Then: controlled parallel execution.
  • Finally: explicitly enroll a real development project.

Keep previous runtime versions and execution-state backups for rollback. Version the state schema and test migrations. Prefer a compatible runtime with the latest execution history. Before restoring an older state backup, stop and confirm affected writers, preserve the newer state and receipts, and reconcile every post-backup assignment and external effect against current artifacts and remote results. Retain cumulative usage, deadlines, ownership versions, finding history and pending operations; a backup must not restore spent allowance or revive an old owner. Resume only with a compatible runtime/state pair and reconciled history. If the missing history cannot be recovered, keep dependent dispatch and writes blocked and report the recovery gap.

Definition of complete

The implementation is complete only when:

  • The document-to-delivery cycle has clear inputs, owners, outputs, and stopping conditions.
  • Existing skills and documents are reused without a second backlog.
  • Each claimed execution mode passes the teamwork compatibility checks, including real hierarchy, supervision, independent QA, and policy-controlled settings.
  • The runner completes multiple approved issues with verified results.
  • Dependencies, review waits, interruptions, and uncertain remote actions behave correctly.
  • Limits and permissions are enforced outside agent prompts.
  • Setup, start, stop, recovery, and upgrade procedures are documented and tested.
  • The final report links actual evidence and clearly identifies anything still unverified.

The result is one shared development process, one GitHub backlog, and one controlled execution service that can be adopted project by project.
